9. Backend specification 
9.1 Structure 
server/src/ 
  app.ts 
  db/          pool, withTenant, scoped 


----- PAGE 81 -----
  middleware/  auth, requireRole, requireSite, validateBody, idempotency 
  config/      registry, graphValidator, compiler, expr, release, merge, diff 
  runtime/     resourceRouter, workflow, stock, excel, sla 
  routes/      hub, sites, builder, runtime, search, demo, health 
  ai/          intent (patch generator), repair loop, fallbackDraft.json 
migrations/    001_base, 002_rls, 003_seed 
scripts/       backup.sh, restore.sh 
tests/         rls, isolation matrix, stock concurrency, graph, expression, idempotency 
 
9.2 Request pipeline 
JWT verify → requireSite (URL site must be in the token's siteIds, else 403) → requireRole (from 
config) → idempotency check (writes) → validateBody (Zod compiled from the release) → 
withTenant (BEGIN, set_config, query, COMMIT) → audit_log 
Rules: site ids come only from the signed token. No route calls pool.query directly (an ESLint rule 
bans it). Every query goes through scoped(siteId) (ORM layer), and RLS is the second layer. 
9.3 Live publish with no restart 
1.​ The builder saves a draft with an optimistic version number. 
2.​ POST /validate runs the checks in 9.4. 
3.​ POST /publish, in one transaction, inserts an immutable release, sets sites.active_release_id, 
and writes a deploy-history row. 
4.​ The runtime resolves site → active release → config on each request. Compiled schemas are 
cached by release id, which is safe because releases never change. 
5.​ The renderer polls /config/version and re-fetches when it changes. 
6.​ Rollback flips the pointer only. 
9.4 Graph validator rules 
Rule 
Example message 
Every node's requires must be present 
"Approval needs a Request node" 
Output type must be accepted by the target input 
"Chart cannot feed Request" 
No cycles except declared workflow loops 
"Cycle: inventory → request → 
inventory" 
Every page role must exist 
"Role 'manager' is not defined" 
Formulas parse, reference real fields, and have no 
cycles 
"total refers to itself" 
9.5 Intent layer (Bolo & Build) 
●​ POST /api/hub/copilot/patch takes {siteSlug, text, lang} and returns a config patch. 


----- PAGE 82 -----
●​ The model is constrained to known module keys, field types and edges via a JSON schema. 
The compiler validates, errors return to the model for up to three rounds, and the response 
includes repairedCount for the UI chip. 
●​ It never publishes. If the API call fails, the endpoint returns fallbackDraft.json. 
9.6 Release diff (Time Slider) 
●​ GET /api/hub/sites/:slug/releases/:a/diff/:b compares two releases and returns changed 
modules, fields, roles and pages as structured items plus a plain-English summary. 
●​ GET /api/hub/sites/:slug/releases/:v/inflight counts non-closed requests grouped by 
config_version. 
●​ GET /api/hub/sites/:slug/releases/:v/config returns any release's config for read-only 
rendering. 
9.7 Expression sandbox 
Parse with jsep, then walk the tree against a whitelist: literals, field names, + - * / %, comparisons, 
&& || !, ternary, and ROUND, MIN, MAX, ABS. Reject member access, constructor, depth above 8, 
and anything else. Tests include qty.constructor and (()=>1)(). 
9.8 Assignment transaction (row locking) 
In one transaction: lock the request (FOR UPDATE, must be APPROVED), lock the inventory row 
(FOR UPDATE), reject if qty_on_hand < qty, update stock, insert a ledger row with the config 
version and idempotency key, insert the assignment, set the request to ASSIGNED. A unique 
violation on the idempotency key returns the original result and never deducts twice.​
 Returns: USABLE adds a RETURN movement (+qty). DAMAGED records the return and adds 
nothing. 
9.9 Offline sync endpoint 
●​ POST /api/:siteId/sync accepts an array of queued actions, each {idempotencyKey, type, 
payload}, limited to request.create and consumption.log. 
●​ Each action runs in its own transaction. The response lists per-action results (SYNCED or 
REJECTED with a reason). A repeated key returns the stored result. 
●​ Stock-changing decisions (approve, assign) are rejected from this endpoint by design. 
9.10 Provisioning endpoint 
POST /api/hub/sites/provision calls create_site and publishes v1 in one transaction, then returns the 
ordered step log and elapsed time. A final probe calls the new site's endpoint as the admin (200) and 
as a user from another site (403) and returns both results. 
9.11 Clash-safe merge 
Save sends base_version. If it equals the draft's current version, save and bump. Otherwise load the 
base snapshot, the current draft (theirs) and the incoming config (mine), flatten each to path/value 


----- PAGE 83 -----
maps, and decide per path: only mine changed → mine; only theirs changed → theirs; both changed to 
the same value → that value; both changed differently → conflict. Return {merged, conflicts[]}. 
9.12 Excel engine 
Export requires the role to be in exportRoles and removes maskColumns[role]. Import requires 
importRoles (otherwise 403), validates every row with the compiled schema, runs all-or-nothing in 
one transaction, and returns row-level errors. Each run is stored in excel_jobs.

9.14 Database design 
Platform tables: users (global identity, bcrypt hash), sites (slug, active release), user_sites (role per 
site), module_catalog (the 13 modules), site_drafts, draft_snapshots, site_releases (immutable 
JSONB), deploy_history. 


----- PAGE 84 -----
Business tables (all have site_id NOT NULL): inventory_items, stock_movements (append-only), 
requests (with status_changed_at and config_version), approvals, assignments, consumption_logs, 
item_returns, pcb_repairs, excel_jobs, audit_log (append-only). 
Small additions for the signature features: 
●​ sync_actions(site_id, idempotency_key, type, status, result jsonb, created_at) with 
UNIQUE(site_id, idempotency_key), so a replayed offline action returns its stored result. 
●​ deploy_history.detail jsonb stores the provisioning step log and timings. 
●​ config_version is stored on requests, stock_movements and audit_log, which powers the 
in-flight panel and the Time Slider. 
Integrity features: 
●​ Composite foreign keys (id, site_id) make cross-site references impossible. 
●​ CHECK (qty_on_hand >= 0) stops negative stock. 
●​ Triggers update status_changed_at on status change and block updates and deletes on the 
ledger and audit log. 
●​ RLS policy on every tenant table: site_id = ANY(app_site_ids()) with WITH CHECK, plus 
FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY. 
●​ A missing session setting fails closed (zero rows) because the policy function uses 
current_setting('app.site_ids', true). 
●​ The app sets the tenant with set_config('app.site_ids', $1, true) inside the transaction, because 
SET LOCAL does not accept bind parameters. 
●​ app_user is NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS, with no update or delete on the ledger and 
audit tables. 
●​ create_site is a SECURITY DEFINER function with a hub-admin check, and the admin's 
token is re-issued afterward so siteIds includes the new site. 
Pre-demo check: SELECT current_user, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 
current_user; must show rolbypassrls = false.

12. Business impact (measured, not claimed) 
●​ Time to provision a new site: __ seconds, 0 server restarts. 
●​ Site-specific code lines: 0 (CI check). 
●​ Reuse: 13 modules × 3 sites, 0 forks. 
●​ Isolation: N / N endpoints tested, no leaks. 
●​ Concurrency: 0 oversells in 50 parallel requests. 
●​ Offline: N queued entries synced, 0 duplicate deductions. 
Who benefits: warehouses, service centres, manufacturers and distributors with several units and 
frequently changing workflows. A process owner changes a form or approval chain without a 
developer, a bad release can be rewound in one drag, and admins get a combined view across units.​
 For Electrolyte: a platform that can onboard a new client site as configuration instead of a new 
project.​
 Do not claim cost savings you haven't measured. 


----- PAGE 87 -----
 
13. Judge Q&A 
●​ "Isn't this just different colours?" No. Three shells and seven layouts are chosen by config, 
and the DOM changes. 
●​ "What if the AI is wrong?" It only drafts a patch. The compiler can reject it, errors feed 
back for repair, a human reviews the diff, and publishing is manual. 
●​ "How is isolation enforced?" In the ORM wrapper and in Postgres RLS under a role that 
can't bypass it. You can try to attack it in the Trust Console. 
●​ "Can offline writes corrupt stock?" No. Offline can only create requests and log 
consumption, and the server decides everything on sync with idempotency keys. 
●​ "What happens to open requests when you roll back?" They keep the config version they 
started under. Rollback moves a pointer and never touches data. 
●​ "Two admins edit at once?" A field-by-field merge resolves non-overlapping edits, and only 
true conflicts need a human. 
●​ "How is this different from Retool or Odoo?" Retool builds single CRUD apps and Odoo 
is a fixed suite. OpsHub assembles different isolated sites from typed modules and releases 
them live. 
●​ "Why these extras if scope is frozen?" Each maps to a required objective, and we've asked 
the guide for approval.

Alignment: 8/10 as written, ~9.5/10 after these fixes. The audit gave 8.5; I dock slightly

1. Problem and what is being judged 
Three units (Central Stores, Service Centre, Distribution) need the same 12 shared 
capabilities with different fields, rules and layouts. Three codebases means 36 
implementations, separate logins, no admin overview, developer-only changes, and risky 
releases with no preview or rollback. 
Expected: one codebase running N different sites in three layers: Hub (JWT + bcrypt, 
RBAC, users, switcher, themes, catalogue), Site ({siteId, slug, theme, modules, 
pages, roles}), and Module/Node (13 frozen typed modules). 
Traps: hardcoded if (site === ...); scope creep past 13 modules; colour-only 
differences; restart needed to publish; JSON-only builder; untyped canvas. 
Winning criteria: site_id isolation at ORM and DB with a test per endpoint; ACID stock 
with row locking; one JSONB-driven renderer; calculated fields with schema validation; the 
rigid four-step demo; seed data and offline backup. 
 
2. Solution in one page 
OpsHub is a configuration brain with a universal body. An admin designs a site by voice, 
typing or a typed canvas. The Brain validates it, compiles it, and saves an immutable 


----- PAGE 91 -----
versioned release. One renderer draws the release; one generic API runs its workflows; 
PostgreSQL isolates every site's data. 
A new site is a new configuration. It never needs new code. 
Pitch: "Three units used to mean 36 implementations. We built 13 modules once, and 
configuration does the rest." 
The moat is the release as the unit of everything: a site is a pointer to an immutable 
release, every transaction is pinned to the config version it ran under, and rollback moves 
one pointer without touching data. 
Positioning: OpsHub is to internal operations software what Shopify is to online stores: 
many visibly different, isolated sites from one multi-tenant codebase, shaped by 
non-developers, with Vercel-style preview, publish and rollback.

Standalone: Session, Role, User, Page, Chart, File 
 
The validator is subtype-aware: an input accepting RequestRecord accepts 
ApprovedRequest. "Any record" for Analytics and Reports means Record.

4. Architecture, data and security 
4.1 Flow 
Voice / typed prompt ─► Intent layer (LLM returns a config PATCH only) 
        │ 
 BRAIN: registry → typed graph validator (subtype-aware) → compiler (Zod + jsep) 
        → immutable release (JSONB) → active-release pointer → diff / impact / rollback 
        │                                   │ 
 Universal renderer                  Generic runtime API 
 (draws any release read-only)       /api/:siteId/:resource  (+ /sync) 
        └──────────────┬────────────────┘ 
 PostgreSQL 16: site_id everywhere · RLS (app_user, NOBYPASSRLS, FORCE) · ledger · 
audit 
        ▲ 
 Phone PWA: IndexedDB cache + write queue + QR       Trust Console: attack · concurrency 
· why 
 
4.2 Stack 
React 18, Vite, TypeScript, Tailwind, React Router, Zustand + IndexedDB, React Flow, 
Recharts, React Hook Form, Zod, jsep, vite-plugin-pwa / Node, Express, JWT, bcrypt, 
ExcelJS, Drizzle ORM / PostgreSQL 16 in Docker. 
ORM (gap closed): Drizzle, wrapped in scoped(siteId), which injects the site_id 
filter. Every request runs inside withTenant, which does BEGIN; 
set_config('app.site_ids', $1, true); …; COMMIT. set_config(..., 
true) is transaction-local, so a pooled connection never carries one request's sites into the 
next. A plain SET would leak. 
4.3 Field storage (gap closed) 
Hybrid tables: typed core columns (site_id, status, qty, status_changed_at, 
config_version) plus a data jsonb column for site-specific fields. Two sites with 
different fields share one table. The compiled Zod schema validates data on every write. 
4.4 Module tables (gap closed) 


----- PAGE 94 -----
Tables for all 13 modules exist in the base migration. Publishing changes only which 
routes and screens are enabled. A resource not in the active release returns 404. 
4.5 Calculated fields (gap closed) 
Expressions are parsed by jsep and checked against a whitelist (literals, field names, + - * 
/ %, comparisons, && || !, ternary, ROUND/MIN/MAX/ABS; depth ≤ 8). The server 
recomputes every calculated field on every write and ignores client-sent values. Test: 
POST a tampered total and confirm it is overwritten. 
4.6 Roles (gap closed) 
Role 
Can do 
Public 
Submit a request through the site's intake endpoint, if the release enables it. 
Nothing else. 
Member 
Create and view own requests, log consumption, view assigned items 
Manager 
Approve and reject, assign stock, run analytics, export unmasked reports 
Admin 
(site) 
Builder, releases, users of that site, import and export, all of the above 
Hub admin 
All sites, provisioning, Trust Console, backup 
Role-per-endpoint comes from the release config. Public intake is the one exception to 
"site id only from the token": the site comes from the slug, the tenant is set to that single 
site, only request.create is allowed, it is rate-limited, and it has its own tests. 
4.7 Tokens (risk closed) 
Access JWT expires in 15 minutes with a refresh token. users.token_version is 
checked on writes and on role-sensitive reads (cached for seconds). Test: revoke a user, 
then confirm their next write fails. 
4.8 Database integrity 
●​ site_id uuid NOT NULL on every business table; composite FKs (id, 
site_id) make cross-site references impossible. 
●​ RLS on every tenant table: site_id = ANY(app_site_ids()) with WITH 
CHECK, plus FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY. A missing setting fails closed (zero 
rows). 
●​ app_user is NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS, with no UPDATE or DELETE on the 
ledger and audit tables. 
●​ CHECK (qty_on_hand >= 0); triggers maintain status_changed_at and block 
ledger and audit mutation. 


----- PAGE 95 -----
●​ Platform tables: users (bcrypt, token_version), sites, user_sites, 
module_catalog, site_drafts, draft_snapshots, site_releases 
(immutable), deploy_history (detail jsonb). 
●​ Business tables: inventory_items, stock_movements, requests, approvals, 
assignments, assignment_lines, consumption_logs, item_returns, 
pcb_repairs, excel_jobs, audit_log, sync_actions (UNIQUE (site_id, 
idempotency_key)). 
●​ config_version on requests, movements and audit rows. 
●​ Pre-demo check: SELECT current_user, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles 
WHERE rolname = current_user; must show false. Run it daily and before 
every demo. 
 
5. Stock integrity (fixes N1, N4) 
Assignment transaction: BEGIN → lock the request (FOR UPDATE, must be APPROVED) → 
lock all inventory rows ordered by item_id (SELECT … ORDER BY item_id FOR 
UPDATE) → reject if any qty_on_hand < qty → update stock → insert ledger rows (config 
version + idempotency key) → insert assignment and lines → set ASSIGNED → COMMIT. A 
unique violation on the idempotency key returns the original result. 
N4: consistent lock order prevents deadlocks on multi-item requests. The concurrency test 
includes multi-item requests that list items in opposite orders. 
N1: ledger reconciliation. Seed data writes opening-balance movements, never 
qty_on_hand directly. A reconciliation query, SUM(movements) = qty_on_hand per 
item, runs in the test suite and is shown on the concurrency panel. 
Returns: USABLE adds a RETURN movement (+qty) when an Inventory node is wired. 
DAMAGED records the return and changes no stock. 
 
6. Releases, rollback and the impact report (fixes N3, 
N5, F4) 
Live publish: draft saved with base_version → POST /validate → POST /publish 
(one transaction: insert immutable release, flip sites.active_release_id, write 
deploy_history). The runtime resolves site → active release → config per request; 
compiled schemas are cached by release id. No restart. 
F4: optimistic locking replaces the merge engine as the default. Every save carries its 
base version; a mismatch returns 409 "draft changed, reload" and loses nothing. Test: two 


----- PAGE 96 -----
tabs, stale save gets 409. The three-way merge is a stretch goal. If built, it must key fields 
by stable field.id (never array index) and treat ordering as its own path. The claim "fixes 
edits to shared code breaking other sites" is dropped: that problem is solved by config/code 
separation, immutable releases and isolation tests. 
N3: rollback safety. Rolling back to a release that lacks fields rows now hold, or the 
reverse, must not strand invalid data. 
●​ Field removal is a soft-hide. Data stays in data jsonb; the renderer hides the 
field. 
●​ Module removal disables routes (404); tables and rows stay. 
●​ Rollback re-validates existing rows against the target release and reports counts of 
rows that do not conform, rather than silently proceeding. 
●​ Test: publish v4 without field X, create rows, roll back to v3, confirm rows still render 
and validate. 
Impact-aware publish (the "I didn't know I needed it" feature). Before Publish enables, 
the Releases page shows the diff between draft and active release with real counts from a 
few queries: "Removes field price (412 rows hold data, hidden not deleted). Adds Returns 
tab. Changes 2 role permissions. 14 open requests keep running under v5." Destructive 
changes need an explicit confirm (confirmDestructive). The same diff function powers 
the Time Slider, so this costs almost nothing extra. 
N5: SLA lives in the release config, per Approval node (sla.seconds). Changing it is a 
publish, not an env var, so "no restart" stays true. The demo release sets 10 s; production 
sets 86400. Say this aloud. 
Pinned execution (honest scope): requests carry config_version. The workflow 
executor loads the pinned release for approval rules and SLA; UI always renders the 
active release. If time is short, show the count and pinned version only.

15. Business impact (measure, do not claim) 
●​ Time to provision a new site: __ s, 0 restarts 
●​ Site-specific code lines: 0 (CI) 
●​ Reuse: 13 modules × 3 sites, 0 forks 
●​ Isolation: N/N endpoints tested, no leaks 
●​ Concurrency: 0 oversells in 50 parallel requests 
●​ Offline: N queued entries synced, 0 duplicate deductions 
Do not claim cost savings you have not measured. 
 
16. Judge Q&A (updated) 
●​ "Isn't this just colours?" No. Three shells and seven layouts come from config and 
the DOM changes. 
●​ "What if the AI is wrong?" It drafts a patch only. The compiler can reject it, errors 
feed back for repair, a human reviews the diff, publishing is manual. 
●​ "How is isolation enforced?" In the ORM wrapper and in Postgres RLS under a 
role that cannot bypass it. Attack it in the Trust Console. 
●​ "Can offline writes corrupt stock?" No. Offline can only ask; the server decides, 
with idempotency keys. 
●​ "What happens to open requests on rollback?" They keep their config version. 
Rollback moves a pointer and never touches data. Removed fields are hidden, not 
deleted, and rollback reports any rows that no longer conform. 
●​ "Two admins edit at once?" The second save gets a 409 and loses nothing. 
●​ "How is this different from Retool or Odoo?" Retool builds single CRUD apps; 
Odoo is a fixed suite. OpsHub assembles different isolated sites from typed modules 
and releases them live. 
●​ "Why these extras if scope is frozen?" Each maps to a required objective. Get 
written approval from the guide, especially for Bolo & Build (LLM dependency) and 
Dead-Zone Mode.

OpsHub: Corrected Solution (v2) 
Genesis 2.0 · PS-03 · Team Pookie_commits. This version closes every finding in the audit 
report. Section 19 maps each finding to its fix and to the test that proves it. 
1. The problem and what is judged 
Three units run on the same basic modules but need different fields, rules and layouts. 
Central Stores handles inventory. Service Centre handles requests, approvals, repairs and 
returns. Distribution handles material requests, assignments and consumption. Today each 
unit has its own codebase, so 12 shared modules become 36 implementations. The results 
are separate logins, no combined admin view, process owners who need a developer to 
change a form, and releases with no preview or rollback. 
Trap 
What fails 
Hardcoded if/else 
per site 
Any if (site === 'service-centre') breaks the core idea. CI greps 
source for site slugs. 
Scope creep 
The 13 modules are frozen. Depth beats breadth. 
Colour-palette trap 
Sites must differ in layout, modules and workflow, not just 
colours. 
Server restart trap 
Publishing must need no code change and no restart. 
Developer-only 
builder 
A raw JSON editor misses the point. 
Untyped canvas 
Approval must not connect without Request. 
Winning criteria: strict site_id isolation at ORM and database level with a test for every 
endpoint; ACID stock transactions with row locking; one universal renderer driven by 
JSONB; calculated fields with schema validation; the rigid four-step demo; seed data and an 
offline backup. 
2. The solution in one page 
OpsHub is a configuration brain with a universal body. An admin designs a site by typing, 
speaking or using the typed canvas. The Brain validates the design, compiles it and saves it 
as an immutable versioned release. One universal renderer turns the release into the 
interface, and one generic API runs the workflows. PostgreSQL keeps every site's data 
isolated. 
Pitch line: “Three units used to mean 36 implementations. We built 13 modules once, and 
configuration does the rest.” 
Positioning: OpsHub is to internal operations software what Shopify is to online stores, with 
Vercel-style preview, publish and rollback. 
The moat is the release as an immutable, versioned unit. A site is a pointer to a release. 
Transactions are pinned to the config version they ran under. Rollback moves one pointer 
and never rewrites business data. 


----- PAGE 109 -----
Honest safety claim: an edit to one site cannot break another because of config-versus-code 
separation, immutable releases, isolation tests and a CI check that no site slug appears in 
source. We do not claim a merge engine solves this.

4. Module registry and type system 
Every module is a typed node. The validator uses requires, input types and output types to 
reject invalid designs. 
Subtype rule: ApprovedRequest is a subtype of RequestRecord. An input that accepts 
RequestRecord also accepts ApprovedRequest, but not the other way round. This makes 
the PS chain Request → Approval → PCB Repair drawable, and it lets approvals chain for 
multi-level sign-off.

Validator rules: every node's requires present; edge types are subtype-aware; no cycles 
except declared workflow loops; every page role exists; formulas parse, reference real fields 
and have no cycles. 
Negative tests: Approval without Request is rejected. Chart into Request is rejected. Material 
Assignment without Inventory is rejected. An Assignment fed by a plain RequestRecord 
(skipping Approval) is rejected. Approval → PCB Repair is accepted. 
5. Architecture and stack 
Voice or typed prompt → Intent layer (the LLM returns a config patch only) → Brain (registry, 
typed graph validator, compiler with Zod and jsep, immutable release in JSONB, 
active-release pointer, release diff, rollback) → Universal renderer and generic runtime API 
(/api/:siteId/:resource) → PostgreSQL (site_id everywhere, RLS under a non-bypass role, 
append-only ledger and audit log). The phone PWA sits beside the renderer with an 
IndexedDB cache. The Trust Console sits beside the runtime API. 
Stack: React 18, Vite, TypeScript, Tailwind, React Router, Zustand with IndexedDB 
persistence, Axios, React Flow, Recharts, React Hook Form, Zod, jsep, vite-plugin-pwa. 
Server: Node, Express, JWT, bcrypt, ExcelJS, Knex wrapped in scoped(trx, siteId), 
PostgreSQL 16 in Docker. If the PS insists on a named ORM, Drizzle drops in behind the 
same scoped() interface. 
Docker Postgres is the primary database because the demo must work without internet and 
the PS requires our own JWT and bcrypt login. The schema is Supabase-compatible, but we 
always connect as app_user. 
Request pipeline: JWT verify (short expiry plus token version) → requireSite (URL site must 
be in the token's siteIds, else 403) → requireRole (from the release config) → idempotency 
check on writes → validateBody (Zod compiled from the release) → withTenant (BEGIN, 
set_config, query through scoped(), COMMIT) → audit log. An ESLint rule bans pool.query 
and bare knex outside db/. 
6. Data model 
Platform tables: users (bcrypt hash, token_version), sites (slug, active_release_id), 
user_sites (role per site), module_catalog, site_drafts, draft_snapshots, site_releases 
(immutable JSONB), deploy_history. 
Business tables, all with site_id NOT NULL: inventory_items, stock_movements 
(append-only ledger), requests, approvals, assignments and assignment_lines, 
consumption_logs, item_returns, pcb_repairs, excel_jobs, sync_actions, audit_log 
(append-only). 


----- PAGE 112 -----
Per-site custom fields. Each business table has typed core columns (site_id, status, qty, 
config_version, status_changed_at) plus a data jsonb column for site-specific fields. Two 
sites with different fields share one table. 
Module tables exist before publish. The base migration creates the tables for all 13 modules. 
Publish never creates or drops tables. It only changes which routes and screens are 
enabled, and a route for a module not in the active release returns 404. 
Removing a field is a soft-hide. The release marks the field hidden, the values stay in data, 
and schemas accept known plus hidden keys. Rows created under v4 therefore still validate 
and render after a rollback to v3. The pre-publish report warns before it happens. 
The ledger always matches stock. qty_on_hand changes only by inserting a 
stock_movements row. Seed data creates OPENING movements instead of writing 
quantities directly. A CHECK keeps qty_on_hand >= 0, and a test asserts SUM(movements) 
= qty_on_hand for every item. 
Integrity extras: composite foreign keys (id, site_id) make cross-site references impossible; 
triggers set status_changed_at and block UPDATE and DELETE on the ledger and audit log; 
config_version is stored on requests, movements and audit rows. 
7. Isolation and sessions 
●​ ORM layer: scoped(trx, siteId) adds site_id to every read and write. Site ids come 
only from the signed token, never from the body. 
●​ Database layer: app_user is NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS. Every tenant table 
has ENABLE and FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY with USING (site_id = ANY 
(app_site_ids())) WITH CHECK (...). app_site_ids() reads 
current_setting('app.site_ids', true), so an unset value returns zero rows and the 
policy fails closed. 
●​ Setting the tenant: BEGIN, then SELECT set_config('app.site_ids', $1, true), then the 
queries, then COMMIT. SET LOCAL cannot take bind parameters, and a plain SET 
leaks one request's sites into the next on a pooled connection. 
●​ Pool-leak test: with the pool size set to 1, two interleaved requests for different sites 
never see each other's rows. 
●​ Session revocation: access tokens expire in 10 minutes. Refresh re-reads user_sites. 
Revoking a user increments users.token_version, which is checked on every request 
with a 5-second cache. A revoked user gets 401 within seconds. 
●​ Pre-demo check: SELECT current_user, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles WHERE 
rolname = current_user; must show rolbypassrls = false. 
8. Roles and permissions 
Per-site roles are Public, Member, Manager and Admin. The Hub admin sits above them. 
The release config defines which roles a site uses, and this matrix is the default.

Public intake is an unauthenticated POST /api/public/:slug/requests. The site must enable it. 
It accepts whitelisted fields only, is rate-limited, creates PENDING requests only and can 
read nothing back. A generated per-role endpoint test table checks every row of this matrix. 
9. Transactions: stock, returns and consumption 
Assignment runs as one transaction for a request with one or more lines: 
1.​ Lock the request with FOR UPDATE. It must be APPROVED. 
2.​ Sort the lines by item id and lock the inventory rows FOR UPDATE in that order. 
Every assignment locks in the same order, so two multi-item assignments cannot 
deadlock. 
3.​ Reject if any qty_on_hand is below the requested quantity. 
4.​ Insert one ASSIGN movement per line (negative quantity, config_version, 
idempotency key), insert the assignment and its lines, and set the request to 
ASSIGNED. 
5.​ A unique violation on the idempotency key returns the original result and never 
deducts twice. 
Returns: USABLE inserts a RETURN movement (+qty) when the site has Inventory. 
DAMAGED records the return and changes no stock. Consumption can never exceed 
assigned minus already consumed. 
Tests: 50 parallel assignments for 10 units give exactly 10 successes, 40 rejections and 
stock 0 with the ledger reconciled. A multi-item run with opposite lock orders finishes with no 
deadlock. 
10. Calculated fields and validation 
●​ Expressions such as qty * price are parsed by jsep and walked against a whitelist: 
literals, field names, + - * / %, comparisons, && || !, ternary, and ROUND, MIN, MAX, 
ABS. Member access, constructor and depth above 8 are rejected. There is no eval. 


----- PAGE 114 -----
●​ The server recomputes every calculated field on every write and overwrites any 
client-sent value. The form shows the live total only as a preview. 
●​ A Zod schema compiled from the release config validates every request body. 
●​ Tests: qty.constructor and (()=>1)() are rejected. A POST with a tampered total 
comes back with the correct total. 
11. Releases, publish and rollback 
Flow. Save a draft with its base_version, validate, preview (a live render of the draft with a 
PREVIEW ribbon), then publish. Publish is one transaction: insert an immutable release, set 
sites.active_release_id, write a deploy_history row. The runtime resolves site → active 
release on every request, and compiled schemas are cached by release id, which is safe 
because releases never change. The renderer polls /config/version every 5 seconds. 
Rollback flips the pointer only. 
Stale-save protection. Each save carries its base_version. A mismatch returns 409 ‘draft 
changed, reload’ together with what changed, so nothing is lost. The field-level merge 
engine is a stretch goal, limited to non-array paths (reordered arrays become conflicts that 
need a human). We never claim it protects shared code. 
Impact-aware publish. Before the Publish button works, it shows the diff between the draft 
and the active release with counts, for example: ‘hides field price (412 rows hold data, 
values kept), adds a Returns tab, changes 2 role permissions, 14 open requests keep 
running under v5’. It is a JSON diff plus a few count queries, and it sits inside the required 
Preview step. 
Config-pinned transactions. Requests, movements and audit rows store config_version. The 
MVP shows the count and the version on each row. Running in-flight requests under their 
pinned release is a stretch goal. 
SLA lives in the release config. Each Approval node carries slaSeconds. Production uses 
86400. For the demo we publish a new version with 10, which needs no restart, and we say 
so aloud. The server sends age_seconds computed from status_changed_at, the client 
re-renders every second with useNow(1000), only PENDING rows can breach, and the 
breach shows as a text badge plus colour. 
Rollback safety. Field removals are soft-hides, so rows created under v4 still render and 
validate under v3. Rollback never changes business rows.

Every feature below is a new experience of a required objective. None is a 14th module.

S3 details. POST /api/hub/copilot/patch takes the text and the module catalogue and returns 
a config patch constrained by a JSON schema. The compiler validates it, and errors go back 
to the model for up to three repair rounds. The AI never publishes. If the API fails, the 


----- PAGE 116 -----
endpoint returns a cached fallbackDraft.json. Voice uses the Web Speech API (hi-IN, mr-IN, 
en-IN), which needs internet and a quiet room, so use a headset, pre-test the exact 
sentences and keep the typed box ready. 
S4 details. Every demo page is pre-warmed into the per-user IndexedDB cache on load, so 
a tab that was never opened is not blank. A /api/health ping runs every 5 seconds, and a 
request that fails with no response counts as offline. The banner reads ‘Offline: viewing 
cached data’, write buttons are disabled with a tooltip, the cache is cleared on logout, and 
vite-plugin-pwa precaches the app shell. Dry-run the stop-and-restart before the demo. 
Stretch: a write queue for create-request and log-consumption only, with idempotency keys 
replayed through POST /api/:siteId/sync. The rule is ‘offline can ask, only the server can 
decide’, so approve and assign stay online-only. QR scanning needs HTTPS, so test a 
tunnel or local certificate on day one. 
S5 details. One transaction runs create_site (a SECURITY DEFINER function with a 
hub-admin check) and publishes v1. The ‘endpoints live’ step is honest: the generic router 
resolves routes from config, so nothing is registered or restarted. 
Supporting features. Ctrl+K search on web and a pull-down sheet on mobile call an 
admin-only GET /api/hub/search?q=, which runs ILIKE with a same-column UNION ALL 
inside withTenant, so RLS limits results to the admin's sites. Results carry origin badges and 
deep-link to /s/:slug/:module?highlight=:id, where the row pulses. The append-only audit log 
feeds the ‘Why?’ drawer.

OpsHub: Final Solution (Merged Edition) 
Genesis 2.0 · PS-03 · Team Pookie_commits Speak a site into existence. Scrub through 
its history. Run it in a dead zone. Try to break it. 
This document merges the three earlier drafts (Corrected v2, Aligned v2, 10/10 Edition), 
keeps the best of each, fixes the defects each one carried, and adds what all three were 
missing.

0.3 What none of the three drafts had (added here) 
1.​ Platform nodes: Auth (module 1) and Dashboard Shell (module 4) are auto-present 
and locked in every site, so "User Management requires Auth" always resolves 
(§4.2). 
2.​ Security hardening checklist: token storage, CORS, helmet, login rate limit, bcrypt 
cost, body limits, upload limits, CSV/Excel formula injection (§16). 
3.​ Excel import and export safety and an all-or-nothing import contract (§15). 


----- PAGE 123 -----
4.​ Demo reset script (npm run demo:reset). Live-publishing Returns changes 
state, so without a reset you cannot rehearse five times (§18). 
5.​ Preview semantics: draft preview is read-only for writes, so it can never mutate data 
(§11). 
6.​ Indexes and pagination so RLS stays fast: (site_id, status), (site_id, 
created_at) (§6). 
7.​ Theme engine as a Hub feature (the PS lists it): tokens become CSS variables per 
site (§13.3). 
8.​ Combined admin view defined precisely: Hub KPIs run in one withTenant over all 
the admin's siteIds (§12.6). 
9.​ Numbers are queried, not hardcoded: "34 of 120" is computed live from the seed 
(§12.2). 
10.​Open questions list for the organizers, because the PS PDF we have stops at the 
Objectives (§24). 
11.​Tooling: Vitest + Supertest, GitHub Actions, docker compose one-command boot 
(§17, §21).

What lifts this to 9.3: no known validator contradictions, every PS objective maps to a test, 
every signature feature has a cut rule and a fallback. 
What keeps it from 10: (a) we have not seen the PS deliverables, rubric or the exact demo 
script; (b) 24 h with 4 people is tight, so the gates are the real protection; (c) voice and LLM 
depend on conditions we do not control; (d) a plan cannot score above its execution. 
Realistic outcome: 9+ if the hour-8 gate holds and the demo is rehearsed. 
 
1. The problem and what is judged 
Three units run on the same basic modules but need different fields, rules and layouts. 
●​ Central Stores: inventory and stock control. 
●​ Service Centre: requests, approvals, repairs and returns. 
●​ Distribution: material requests, assignments and consumption. 


----- PAGE 124 -----
Each unit has its own codebase, so 12 modules × 3 units = 36 implementations where 12 
shared would do. Consequences: separate logins, no combined admin view, process owners 
need a developer to change a form, edits to shared code can break another site, and 
releases have no preview or rollback. 
Expected: one codebase running N different sites in three layers: Hub (JWT + bcrypt, 
RBAC, users, switcher, theme engine, module catalogue), Site ({siteId, slug, theme, 
modules, pages, roles}), Module/Node (13 frozen typed modules). 
Trap

workflow

restart

Untyped canvas 
Approval must not connect without Request 
Winning criteria: strict site_id isolation at ORM and DB level with a test per endpoint; 
ACID stock with row locking; one JSONB-driven renderer; calculated fields with schema 
validation; the rigid four-step demo; seed data and an offline backup. 
 
2. The solution in one page 
OpsHub is a configuration brain with a universal body. An admin designs a site by 
typing, speaking, or using the typed canvas. The Brain validates the design, compiles it, and 
saves an immutable versioned release. One universal renderer draws the release. One 
generic API runs the workflows. PostgreSQL isolates every site's data. A new site is a new 
configuration, never new code. 
Pitch line: "Three units used to mean 36 implementations. We built 13 modules once, and 
configuration does the rest." 
Positioning: OpsHub is to internal operations software what Shopify is to online stores, with 
Vercel-style preview, publish and rollback. 


----- PAGE 125 -----
The moat: the release is an immutable, versioned unit. A site is a pointer to a release. 
Transactions are pinned to the config version they ran under. Rollback moves one pointer 
and never rewrites business data. 
Honest safety claim: an edit to one site cannot break another because of 
config-versus-code separation, immutable releases, isolation tests, and a CI check that no 
site slug appears in source.

4. Module registry and type system 
4.1 Types and subtyping 
Record (abstract) 
├── StockItem 
├── RequestRecord 
│   └── ApprovedRequest      (subtype of RequestRecord) 
├── Assignment 
├── ConsumptionRecord 
├── ReturnRecord 
└── RepairRecord 
Standalone: Session, Role, User, Page, Chart, File 
 
An input accepting RequestRecord also accepts ApprovedRequest, never the reverse. 
This makes Request → Approval → PCB Repair drawable and lets approvals chain for 
multi-level sign-off. "Any record" for Analytics and Reports means Record. 


----- PAGE 127 -----
4.2 The 13 modules 
A node's requires is satisfied only when a compatible edge actually connects, not 
merely when the node exists on the canvas. Modules 1 and 4 are platform nodes: 
auto-present and locked in every site.

Service Centre has no Inventory, so its Returns is not wired to stock. Distribution has

ledger. 
4.4 Validator rules 
1.​ Every node's requires has a compatible connected edge. 
2.​ Edge types are subtype-aware. 
3.​ No cycles except declared workflow loops. 
4.​ Every page role exists. 
5.​ Formulas parse, reference real fields, and have no cycles. 
6.​ Platform nodes cannot be deleted. 
Negative tests (CI): Approval without Request → rejected. Chart into Request → rejected. 
Material Assignment without Inventory → rejected. Assignment fed by a plain 
RequestRecord (skipping Approval) → rejected. Inventory → Returns → rejected. 
Approval → PCB Repair → accepted. CI job validate-seeds loads all three presets; the 
build fails if any is rejected. 
 
5. Architecture and stack 
Voice / typed prompt ─► Intent layer (LLM returns a config PATCH only) 
        │ 
 BRAIN: registry → typed graph validator (subtype-aware) → compiler (Zod + jsep) 
        → immutable release (JSONB) → active-release pointer → diff / impact / rollback 
        │                                   │ 
 Universal renderer                  Generic runtime API 
 (draws any release read-only)       /api/:siteId/:resource 
        └──────────────┬────────────────┘ 
 PostgreSQL 16: site_id everywhere · RLS (app_user, NOBYPASSRLS, FORCE) · ledger · 
audit 


----- PAGE 129 -----
        ▲                                      ▲ 
 Phone PWA: IndexedDB cache (+ queue stretch)  Trust Console: attack · concurrency · why 
 
Frontend: React 18, Vite, TypeScript, Tailwind, React Router, Zustand (+ IndexedDB 
persistence), Axios, React Flow, Recharts, React Hook Form, Zod, jsep, vite-plugin-pwa. 
Backend: Node, Express, JWT, bcrypt, ExcelJS, Drizzle ORM wrapped in scoped(tx, 
siteId), PostgreSQL 16 in Docker. Tests: Vitest, Supertest. CI: GitHub Actions. 
Why Docker Postgres: the demo must work offline and the PS requires our own JWT + 
bcrypt. The schema is Supabase-compatible, but we always connect as app_user. 
Request pipeline: JWT verify (10 min + token_version) → requireSite (URL 
site must be in token siteIds, else 403) → requireRole (from release config) → 
idempotency check (writes) → validateBody (Zod compiled from release) → 
withTenant (BEGIN, set_config, query via scoped(), COMMIT) → audit log. 
An ESLint rule bans pool.query and raw ORM clients outside db/. 
 
6. Data model 
Platform tables: users (bcrypt hash, token_version), sites (slug, 
active_release_id), user_sites (role per site), module_catalog, site_drafts, 
draft_snapshots, site_releases (immutable JSONB), deploy_history (detail 
jsonb). 
Business tables (all site_id uuid NOT NULL): inventory_items, 
stock_movements (append-only ledger), requests, approvals, assignments, 
assignment_lines, consumption_logs, item_returns, pcb_repairs, 
excel_jobs, sync_actions (UNIQUE (site_id, idempotency_key)), audit_log 
(append-only). 
Per-site custom fields. Each business table has typed core columns (site_id, status, 
qty, config_version, status_changed_at) plus a data jsonb column for 
site-specific fields. Two sites with different fields share one table. 
Module tables exist before publish. The base migration creates tables for all 13 modules. 
Publish never creates or drops tables; it changes which routes and screens are enabled. A 
route for a module not in the active release returns 404. 
Field removal is a soft-hide. The release marks the field hidden, values stay in data, and 
schemas accept known plus hidden keys. Rows created under v4 still validate and render 
after rollback to v3. 


----- PAGE 130 -----
The ledger always matches stock. qty_on_hand changes only through an insert into 
stock_movements (an AFTER INSERT trigger applies it). Seed data creates OPENING 
movements, never writes quantities directly. CHECK (qty_on_hand >= 0). A test asserts 
SUM(movements) = qty_on_hand for every item. 
Integrity extras: 
●​ Composite foreign keys (id, site_id) make cross-site references impossible. 
●​ Triggers set status_changed_at and block UPDATE/DELETE on ledger and audit 
log. 
●​ config_version stored on requests, movements and audit rows. 
●​ Indexes: (site_id, status), (site_id, created_at), (site_id, 
item_id). Lists are paginated, so RLS stays fast. 
 
7. Isolation and sessions 
ORM layer. scoped(tx, siteId) adds site_id to every read and write. Site ids come 
only from the signed token, never from the body. 
Database layer. app_user is NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS. Every tenant table has 
ENABLE and FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY with USING (site_id = ANY 
(app_site_ids())) and WITH CHECK (...). app_site_ids() reads 
current_setting('app.site_ids', true), so an unset value returns zero rows (fails 
closed). 
Setting the tenant. BEGIN; SELECT set_config('app.site_ids', $1, true); 
…queries…; COMMIT;. SET LOCAL cannot take bind parameters, and a plain SET leaks 
one request's sites into the next on a pooled connection. 
Pool-leak test. With pool size 1, two interleaved requests for different sites never see each 
other's rows. 
Session revocation. Access tokens expire in 10 minutes. Refresh re-reads user_sites. 
Revoking a user increments users.token_version, checked on every request with a 
5-second cache. A revoked user gets 401 within seconds. 
Pre-demo check (run daily and before every rehearsal): 
SELECT current_user, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user; 
-- must show rolbypassrls = false, otherwise the isolation proof is fake 
 
 


----- PAGE 131 -----

9. Transactions: stock, returns, consumption 
Assignment runs as one transaction for a request with one or more lines: 
1.​ Lock the request FOR UPDATE. It must be APPROVED. 
2.​ Sort lines by item id and lock inventory rows FOR UPDATE in that order. Every 
assignment locks in the same order, so two multi-item assignments cannot deadlock. 
3.​ Reject if any qty_on_hand is below the requested quantity. 
4.​ Insert one ASSIGN movement per line (negative qty, config_version, 
idempotency key), insert the assignment and lines, set request to ASSIGNED. 
5.​ A unique violation on the idempotency key returns the original result and never 
deducts twice. 


----- PAGE 132 -----
Returns: USABLE inserts a RETURN movement (+qty) when the site has Inventory wired. 
DAMAGED records the return and changes no stock. 
Consumption can never exceed assigned minus already consumed. 
Tests: 50 parallel assignments for 10 units → exactly 10 successes, 40 rejections, stock 0, 
ledger reconciled. A multi-item run with opposite lock orders finishes with no deadlock. 
 
10. Calculated fields and validation 
Expressions such as qty * price are parsed by jsep and walked against a whitelist: 
literals, field names, + - * / %, comparisons, && || !, ternary, and ROUND, MIN, MAX, 
ABS. Member access, constructors, and depth above 8 are rejected. No eval. 
The server recomputes every calculated field on every write and overwrites any client 
value. The form shows the live total only as a preview. 
A Zod schema compiled from the release config validates every request body. 
Tests: qty.constructor and (()=>1)() are rejected. A POST with a tampered total 
comes back with the correct total. 
 
11. Releases, preview, publish and rollback 
Flow: save draft with base_version → validate → preview (live render of the draft with a 
PREVIEW ribbon) → publish. Publish is one transaction: insert an immutable release, set 
sites.active_release_id, write a deploy_history row. The runtime resolves site → 
active release on every request; compiled schemas are cached by release id (safe, releases 
never change). The renderer polls /config/version every 5 s. Rollback flips the 
pointer only. 
Preview semantics. Preview renders the draft config against the site's real data read-only. 
All write buttons are disabled in preview, so preview can never mutate data. 
Stale-save protection. Each save carries base_version. A mismatch returns 409 "draft 
changed, reload" with what changed, so nothing is lost. Field-level merge (non-array paths 
only, fields keyed by stable field.id) is a stretch goal. 
Impact-aware publish. Before Publish is enabled, it shows the diff between draft and active 
release with real counts, for example: "hides field price (412 rows hold data, values kept), 
adds a Returns tab, changes 2 role permissions, 14 open requests keep running under v5." 


----- PAGE 133 -----
It is a JSON diff plus a few count queries, and it sits inside the required Preview step. 
Destructive changes need an explicit confirmDestructive. 
Config-pinned transactions. Requests, movements and audit rows store 
config_version. MVP shows the count and version on each row. Running in-flight 
requests under their pinned release is a stretch goal. 
SLA lives in the release config. Each Approval node carries slaSeconds. Production 
uses 86400. For the demo we publish a new version with 10, which needs no restart, and 
we say so aloud. The server sends age_seconds from status_changed_at; the client 
re-renders every second; only PENDING rows can breach; the breach shows as a text 
badge plus colour. 
Rollback safety. Field removals are soft-hides. Module removal disables routes (404) while 
tables and rows stay. Rollback never changes business rows and reports how many rows no 
longer conform to the target release.

12.1 S1 Release Rewind 
Releases are immutable JSONB, so the renderer can draw any release read-only. The slider 
swaps the config the preview renders. A diff function summarises changes in words. The 
same diff powers impact-aware publish, so it costs almost nothing extra. Rollback is a 
pointer flip. Line to say: "It's gone, it's back, and nothing in the database changed." 
12.2 S2 Trust Console 
●​ Isolation. A forged site id gets 403 from Express. Then a query with no WHERE in 
code returns, for example, 34 of 120 rows as a dot grid, which is Postgres filtering. 
Show them as two separate proofs. Counts are queried live from the seed, never 
hardcoded. A psql check without the setting returns 0 rows. 
●​ Concurrency. One button fires 50 parallel assignments for 10 units: 10 OK, 40 
rejected, stock 0, ledger balanced. 
●​ Why? Every rejection has a drawer with the decision trace: rule, inputs, config 
version (from the append-only audit log). 
12.3 S3 Bolo & Build 
POST /api/hub/copilot/patch takes {siteSlug, text, lang} and the module 
catalogue, and returns a config patch constrained by a JSON schema (known module keys, 
field types and edges only). The compiler validates; errors go back to the model for up to 
three repair rounds; repairedCount drives the chip. The AI drafts. The compiler 
checks. A human publishes. If the API fails, the endpoint returns a cached 
fallbackDraft.json. 
Voice uses the Web Speech API (hi-IN, mr-IN, en-IN). It needs internet and a quiet 
room, so use a headset, pre-test the exact sentences, and keep the typed box ready. 
Demo sentence (Distribution): "Distribution mein returns jodo. Kharab saman inspection ke 
baad hi wapas stock mein aaye." English fallback: "Add Returns to Distribution. Damaged 
items must not go back into stock." Another: "approval chain with two approvers above 
₹25,000" → Approval with total > 25000 → 2 approvals. 
12.4 S4 Dead-Zone Lite 
Every demo page is pre-warmed into the per-user IndexedDB cache on load, so a 
never-opened tab is not blank. A /api/health ping every 5 s, and any request that fails 
with no response, counts as offline. The banner reads "Offline: viewing cached data", write 
buttons are disabled with a tooltip, the cache is cleared on logout, and vite-plugin-pwa 
precaches the app shell. Dry-run the stop-and-restart before the demo. 
Stretch: a write queue for request.create and consumption.log only, replayed 
through POST /api/:siteId/sync with idempotency keys. Rule: offline can ask, only 
the server can decide, so approve and assign stay online-only. QR scanning and PWA 
install need HTTPS (localhost is exempt), so test a tunnel or local certificate on day one. 


----- PAGE 135 -----
12.5 S5 One-Save Provisioning 
One transaction runs create_site (a SECURITY DEFINER function with a hub-admin 
check) and publishes v1; the admin's token is re-issued so siteIds includes the new site. 
The "endpoints live" step is honest: the generic router resolves routes from config, so 
nothing is registered or restarted. The last step probes the new site (200 for its admin, 403 
for another site's user). 
12.6 Supporting features 
●​ Combined admin view. Hub KPIs (sites, open requests, SLA breaches, low stock) 
run in one withTenant over all of the admin's siteIds. 
●​ Ctrl+K cross-site search. GET /api/hub/search?q= runs ILIKE with a 
same-column UNION ALL inside withTenant, so RLS limits results to the admin's 
sites. Results carry origin badges and deep-link to 
/s/:slug/:module?highlight=:id, where the row pulses. 
●​ Aging SLA rings (§11), audit log + "Why?" drawer, config-pinned transactions. 
12.7 Optional: public intake (stretch) 
Unauthenticated POST /api/public/:slug/requests, only if the release enables it. 
Whitelisted fields only, rate-limited, creates PENDING requests only, reads nothing back. It is 
the one exception to "site id only from the token": the tenant is set to that single site, and 
only request.create is allowed. Has its own tests. Build only after M1–M10.

15. Excel import and export 
●​ Export requires the role in exportRoles and removes maskColumns[role] 
(Member gets masked columns). 
●​ Import requires importRoles (otherwise 403), validates every row with the 
compiled schema, runs all-or-nothing in one transaction, and returns row-level 
errors. Each run is stored in excel_jobs. 
●​ Safety (added): cap file size and row count, accept .xlsx only, reject macros, and 
prefix cell values beginning with =, +, -, @ with an apostrophe on export to prevent 
formula injection.

Discipline 
●​ npm run demo:reset restores the seed state (v1 of all sites, Returns unpublished 
on Distribution) so you can rehearse repeatedly. 
●​ Rehearse five times with a stopwatch and a headset mic, using the PS's own site 
names and URLs (/central-stores, /service-centre, /distribution). 
●​ Record a fallback video of the full run. Keep backup restore one command away. 
●​ Run the rolbypassrls check before every run. 
●​ If time is tight: drop Dead-Zone first, then shorten the Trust Console. One-Save 
Provisioning is a Q&A clip.

20. Team, build order and cut order 


----- PAGE 143 -----
Team (4): A Data & Security (schema, RLS, withTenant, stock, backup, isolation tests, 
Trust Console endpoints) · B Brain & Builder (registry, validator, jsep, releases, diff, intent 
layer, React Flow) · C Renderer & Runtime UI (shells, layouts, runtime pages, SLA ring, 
analytics, Excel UI, Rewind rendering) · D Hub, Proof & Demo (Hub pages, Trust Console 
UI, provisioning log, search, PWA cache, deck, script, video). 
Shared contract (hour 0–1): only withTenant/scoped touch the DB. JWT carries 
userId, hubRole, siteIds[], per-site roles. Every tenant table has site_id + RLS. 
Requests always have status, status_changed_at, config_version. The config 
shape is the only Brain↔Renderer contract. Every runtime page reads ?highlight=<id>. 
Logout calls clearCache(). 
Phases (PS order): hours 0–8 auth, hub, switcher, site CRUD, inventory, multi-tenant 
schema, RLS → gate. Hours 8–16 request → approval → assignment with stock, calculated 
fields, typed canvas, publish and rollback. Hours 16–21 consumption, returns, repair kanban, 
analytics, Excel, then S1 and S2. Hours 21–24 freeze, five rehearsals, fallback video. 
Build order: M1–M8 foundations → S1 Rewind → S2 Trust Console → S3 typed co-pilot → 
S5 Provisioning → S4 Dead-Zone Lite → voice, queue, merge, QR. 
Cut order if time slips: merge engine (keep 409) → Dead-Zone beyond banner + cached 
reads → Ctrl+K search → voice (keep typed co-pilot and cached draft) → AI co-pilot entirely. 
Never cut: the gate, RLS with a non-bypass role, typed canvas, live publish without restart, 
transactional stock, role-restricted Excel, backup and restore, Release Rewind, Trust 
Console, the four-step rehearsal, the fallback video.

22. Business impact (measure, do not claim) 
●​ Time to provision a new site: __ s, 0 restarts 
●​ Site-specific code lines: 0 (CI) 
●​ Reuse: 13 modules × 3 sites, 0 forks 
●​ Isolation: N/N endpoints tested, no leaks 
●​ Concurrency: 0 oversells in 50 parallel requests 
Who benefits: multi-unit warehouses, service centres and distributors whose workflows 
keep changing. A process owner edits a form or approval chain without a developer, a bad 
release is rewound in one drag, and admins get a combined view. For Electrolyte: 
onboarding a new client site becomes configuration, not a new project. Do not claim cost 
savings you have not measured. 
 
23. Judge Q&A 
●​ "Isn't this just colours?" No. Three shells and four layouts come from config and 
the DOM changes. 
●​ "What if the AI is wrong?" It drafts a patch only. The compiler can reject it, errors 
feed back for repair, a human reviews the diff, publishing is manual. 
●​ "How is isolation enforced?" In the ORM wrapper and in Postgres RLS under a 
role that cannot bypass it. Attack it in the Trust Console. 
●​ "Can a bad edit break another site?" Config and code are separate, releases are 
immutable, every site is isolated by tests, and CI forbids site slugs in source. 
●​ "What happens to open requests on rollback?" They keep their config version. 
Rollback moves a pointer and never touches data. Removed fields are hidden, not 
deleted, and rollback reports rows that no longer conform. 
●​ "Two admins edit at once?" The second save gets a 409 and loses nothing. 
●​ "Can offline corrupt stock?" No. Offline can only ask; the server decides, with 
idempotency keys. 
●​ "Retool or Odoo?" Retool builds single CRUD apps; Odoo is a fixed suite. OpsHub 
assembles different isolated sites from typed modules and releases them live. 
●​ "Why extras if scope is frozen?" Each one is a new experience of a required 
objective, not a new module. 
 
24. Open questions to confirm with the organizers 
The PS PDF we have stops at the Objectives list. Confirm before the build starts: 
1.​ The remaining PS-03 text: deliverables, judging rubric and the exact demo script. 
2.​ Which site hosts the live-publish demo (this plan assumes Distribution gets Returns) 
and whether the sandbox or stack has constraints. 


----- PAGE 145 -----
3.​ Whether the auto-approve Approval node on Distribution is acceptable, since the PS 
example reads "request, then assignment". 
4.​ Whether Drizzle counts as the expected ORM. 
5.​ Written approval for Bolo & Build (LLM dependency) and Dead-Zone Lite. 
6.​ Whether a Public (unauthenticated) role is expected at all. This plan treats it as 
optional.

1. The problem and what the organizer is judging 
1.1 The problem 
A company runs three units: Central Stores (inventory), Service Centre (requests, 
approvals, repairs, returns) and Distribution (material requests, assignments, 
consumption). All need the same basic features but want different fields, rules and layouts, 
so each gets its own codebase. 3 units × 12 modules = 36 implementations where 12 
shared ones would do. 
Consequences: separate logins, no combined admin view, process owners who need a 
developer to change a form or approval chain, shared-code edits that break other sites, and 
releases with no preview or rollback. 
1.2 What the organizer expects 
One codebase running N completely different sites in three layers: 
●​ Hub: single app shell, global JWT + bcrypt login, RBAC, user management, site 
switcher, theme engine, module catalogue. 
●​ Site: { siteId, slug, theme, modules, pages, roles }. 
●​ Module and node: 13 approved modules as typed nodes with defined inputs and 
outputs. 
1.3 The traps that disqualify 
Trap

greps for slugs 
Scope creep 
The 13 modules are frozen. Depth beats breadth

Sites must differ in layout, modules and workflow, not just colours 


----- PAGE 148 -----

1.4 The winning criteria 
Strict site_id isolation at ORM and database level with a test for every endpoint · ACID 
stock with row locking · one universal renderer over JSONB · calculated fields with schema 
validation · a rigid four-step demo · seeded data and an offline backup.

2. Our solution in one page 
OpsHub is a configuration brain with a universal body. 
An admin designs a site by voice, by typing, or on a typed canvas. The Brain validates the 
design, compiles it, and saves it as an immutable versioned release. One universal 
renderer turns the release into the interface. One generic API runs the workflows. 
PostgreSQL keeps every site's data isolated. 
The promise: a new site is a new configuration. It never needs new code. 
The pitch line: "Three units used to mean 36 implementations. We built 13 modules once, 
and configuration does the rest." 
What OpsHub is famous for: 
The platform where you speak a business into existence, scrub through its 
history like a video, keep it running in a warehouse dead zone, and invite you to 
try to break it. 
Product people 
remember 
One-sentence hook 
OpsHub's equivalent 
ResQLink 
Works with no 
internet 
Dead-Zone Lite: every demo page still 
renders offline 
Kisaan Vaani 
Talk to an AI in your 
language 
Bolo & Build: describe a process in Hindi, 
Marathi or English 
Instagram Reels 
Scrub, swipe, watch 
Release Rewind: drag a slider, the live site 
morphs 
The moat: the release is an immutable, versioned unit. A site is a pointer to a release. 
Transactions are pinned to the config version they ran under. Rollback moves one pointer 
and never rewrites business data. 
Honest safety claim: one site's edit cannot break another because of config-versus-code 
separation, immutable releases, isolation tests, and a CI check that no site slug appears in 
source. We do not claim a merge engine solves this. 
Positioning: OpsHub is to internal operations software what Shopify is to online stores, with 
Vercel-style preview, publish and rollback.

Where we differ

Scope rule: the 13 modules are frozen. Every signature feature is a new experience of a

3. Architecture 
Voice / typed prompt ──► Intent layer (LLM returns a config PATCH only) 
                                   │ 
 ┌──────────────── BRAIN (config engine) ────────────────┐ 
 │ Module registry → Typed graph validator (subtype-aware)│ 
 │ → Compiler (Zod + jsep) → Immutable release (JSONB) →  │ 
 │ Active-release pointer · Release diff · Impact · Rollback│ 
 └───────────┬──────────────────────────┬────────────────┘ 
             ▼                          ▼ 
   Universal renderer            Generic runtime API 
   (also renders any past        /api/:siteId/:resource 
    release read-only) 
             └──────────┬───────────────┘ 
                        ▼ 
 PostgreSQL 16: site_id everywhere · RLS (app_user, NOBYPASSRLS, FORCE) 
                · append-only ledger and audit log 
                        ▲ 
 Phone PWA: IndexedDB cache (+ write queue, stretch) 
 Trust Console: attack editor · concurrency · decision trace 
 
Layer

Stack: React 18, Vite, TypeScript, Tailwind, React Router, Zustand + IndexedDB 
persistence, Axios, React Flow, Recharts, React Hook Form, Zod, jsep, vite-plugin-pwa / 
Node, Express, JWT, bcrypt, ExcelJS, Drizzle ORM wrapped in scoped(tx, siteId) / 
PostgreSQL 16 in Docker. Tests: Vitest + Supertest. CI: GitHub Actions. 
Postgres or Supabase? Docker Postgres is primary: the demo must work offline and the 
PS needs our own JWT and bcrypt login. The schema is Supabase-compatible, but we 
always connect as app_user, because default roles can bypass RLS. 
Request pipeline: JWT verify (10 min + token_version) → requireSite (URL site 
must be in token siteIds, else 403) → requireRole (from release config) → 
idempotency check (writes) → validateBody (Zod compiled from release) → 
withTenant (BEGIN, set_config, query through scoped(), COMMIT) → audit log. An 
ESLint rule bans raw pool.query outside db/. 
 
4. The 13 modules (the building blocks) 
Every module is a typed node. The validator uses requires, input types and output types 
to reject invalid designs. 
Type tree: Record → StockItem, RequestRecord (→ ApprovedRequest), 
Assignment, ConsumptionRecord, ReturnRecord, RepairRecord. Standalone: 
Session, Role, User, Page, Chart, File. An input accepting RequestRecord also 
accepts ApprovedRequest, never the reverse. "Any record" means Record. 
A node's requires is satisfied only when a compatible edge actually connects. Modules 
1 and 4 are platform nodes: auto-present and locked in every site.

Validator rules: every requires has a connected compatible edge · edge types are 
subtype-aware · no cycles except declared workflow loops · every page role exists · 
formulas parse, reference real fields, have no cycles · platform nodes cannot be deleted. 
Negative tests: Approval without Request, Chart into Request, Material Assignment without 
Inventory, Assignment fed by a plain RequestRecord, and Inventory → Returns are all 
rejected. Approval → PCB Repair and Request → Returns are accepted. 
 
5. Signature features (S+): why people will remember 
us 
Signature 1: Release Rewind (Time Slider) 
What judges see. On the Releases page, a slider with one tick per version (v1 … v5). The 
presenter drags back. The live site morphs: the Returns tab disappears, a field vanishes, the 
layout shifts. Drag forward and it returns. A plain-English report appears: "v5 → v4: removes 
1 module and 2 fields. 14 open requests keep running under v5." A Rollback button commits 
the choice. 
How it works. 
●​ Releases are immutable JSONB, so the renderer can draw any release read-only. 
The slider swaps the config the preview renders. 
●​ A diff function summarises changes in words. The same function powers 
impact-aware publish, so it is almost free. 
●​ The in-flight panel counts open requests per config_version. 
●​ Rollback flips the active-release pointer and never touches business data. 


----- PAGE 154 -----
Importance. It turns the dullest required objective (preview, versions, rollback, history) into 
the most visual moment of the demo, and answers the organizer's complaint directly: "with 
no preview or rollback, every release is risky." 
Example line. "It's gone. It's back. Nothing in the database changed." 
Risk. Running in-flight requests under their pinned release is a stretch goal. MVP shows the 
count and the pinned version. 
Maps to: preview, versioned releases, rollback, deploy history. Cost: ~4 h. Cut rule: never 
cut (fallback: diff without the morph animation). 
Signature 2: Trust Console ("Try to break it") 
What judges see. A Hub page where the judge is invited to attack, in three panels: 
1.​ Isolation. A forged site id or tampered token gets 403 from Express. Below it, a 
query with no WHERE clause in code returns a dot grid of the seeded rows with 
only the user's lit (for example 34 of 120). That is Postgres filtering, not the app. 
Counts are queried live. A psql check with no setting returns 0 rows. 
2.​ Concurrency. One button fires 50 parallel assignments for 10 units: 10 succeed, 40 
rejected, stock 0, ledger balanced. 
3.​ Why was I blocked? Every rejection has a drawer with its decision trace: rule, 
inputs, config version. 
How it works. Demo-mode-only endpoints (DEMO_MODE=true), app_user with RLS, FOR 
UPDATE row locks, idempotency keys, the append-only audit log. Show the 403 and the 
filtering as two separate proofs. 
Importance. Isolation and transactional integrity are the PS's core winning criteria, and most 
teams will only claim them. We make them tangible. Inviting an attack is a confidence signal 
almost no team sends. 
Risk. Check rolbypassrls = false before every demo, or the proof is fake. 
Maps to: per-endpoint isolation tests and transactional integrity. Cost: ~5 h. Cut rule: never 
cut. 
Signature 3: Bolo & Build (describe a process, in your language) 
What judges see. The presenter types, or taps the mic and says: "Distribution mein returns 
jodo. Kharab saman inspection ke baad hi wapas stock mein aaye." On the canvas, nodes 
appear one by one: Request → Returns, with an inspection field and a USABLE/DAMAGED 
rule. The validator ticks each green. If the AI erred, a chip says "fixed 1 issue automatically". 
The presenter reviews the diff and presses Publish. English fallback: "Add Returns to 
Distribution. Damaged items must not go back into stock." 
How it works. 


----- PAGE 155 -----
●​ POST /api/hub/copilot/patch takes {siteSlug, text, lang} plus the 
module catalogue and returns a config patch constrained by a JSON schema (known 
module keys, field types and edges only). 
●​ The compiler validates. Errors go back to the model for up to three repair rounds. 
repairedCount drives the chip. 
●​ The AI drafts. The compiler checks. A human publishes. If the API fails, a cached 
fallbackDraft.json loads. 
●​ Voice uses the Web Speech API (hi-IN, mr-IN, en-IN). The typed box uses the 
same path. 
Example. "approval chain with two approvers above ₹25,000" → Approval with total > 
25000 → 2 approvals, evaluated by the same jsep sandbox. 
Importance. The strongest answer to "a builder only developers can use misses the point". 
It makes live-publish memorable, and Hindi/Marathi input in Mumbai is a local-relevance 
moment. 
Risks. Speech needs internet and a quiet room: use a headset, pre-test the exact 
sentences, keep the typed box and cached draft ready. 
Maps to: non-developer builder objective. Cost: ~4 h typed, +2 h voice. Cut rule: cut voice 
first; the typed co-pilot stays. 
Signature 4: Dead-Zone Lite (works with no network) 
What judges see. On a real phone with the PWA installed, the presenter stops the server or 
switches on airplane mode. An "Offline: viewing cached data" banner appears and every 
demo page still renders from cache. Write buttons are disabled with a tooltip. 
How it works. 
●​ Every demo page is pre-warmed into the per-user IndexedDB cache on load, so a 
never-opened tab is not blank. 
●​ /api/health pings every 5 s; a request that fails with no response counts as 
offline. 
●​ vite-plugin-pwa precaches the app shell. The cache is cleared on logout. 
Stretch. A write queue for request.create and consumption.log only, replayed 
through POST /api/:siteId/sync with idempotency keys. Rule: offline can ask, only 
the server can decide, so approve and assign stay online-only. QR scan on top. 
Risks. Camera and PWA install need HTTPS (localhost exempt): test a tunnel or local 
certificate on day one. Dry-run the stop-and-restart before the demo. 
Maps to: the offline backup deliverable and Consumption/Return inputs. Cost: ~3 h (lite). 
Cut rule: first to cut beyond the banner and cached reads; the real pg_dump backup stays 
regardless. 


----- PAGE 156 -----
Signature 5: One-Save Provisioning 
What judges see. Create a site from a preset and save once. A live log ticks through real 
steps: site registered, draft seeded, config validated, release v1 published, roles assigned, 
endpoints live, isolation verified. A timer shows the total. The last step probes the new site: 
200 for its admin, 403 for another site's user. 
How it works. One transaction runs create_site (a SECURITY DEFINER function with a 
hub-admin check) and publishes v1. The admin's token is re-issued so siteIds includes 
the new site. "Endpoints live" is honest: the generic router resolves routes from config, so 
nothing is registered or restarted. 
Importance. The PS says one builder puts together completely different sites, each with its 
own isolated backend, in a single save. This is that sentence made visible. 
Maps to: the single-save objective. Cost: ~2.5 h. Cut rule: cut second; use it as a 
20-second Q&A clip.

6. Foundations (MVP): the baseline that must be 
flawless 
Nobody remembers these, but missing any one loses points or the whole event. 
F1: One login, many sites, a Hub admin view 
What: One login issues a JWT with siteIds and per-site roles. A site switcher loads each 
site with its own theme, pages and data. Hub KPIs run in one withTenant over all of the 


----- PAGE 157 -----
admin's sites. Example: log in once, see all three units, enter Service Centre, no second 
login. Importance: removes "separate logins, no combined view"; demo step 1. 
F2: One universal renderer 
What: One React renderer reads the site's JSONB config and produces shell, layout, forms 
and tables. No site-specific React files. Example: Central Stores draws a sidebar and stock 
table; Service Centre draws a top bar and a kanban; same renderer. Importance: the heart 
of the PS. CI fails if a site slug appears in source. 
F3: Layout-changing sites 
What: Config selects one of three shells (sidebar, topbar-cards, workbench) and one of four 
layouts (table, kanban, split, dashboard); forms are shared; theme tokens are the smallest 
difference. Example: Distribution is a split pane with a stock availability meter; Central 
Stores is a plain table. Importance: the organizer says swapping CSS variables is not 
enough; the DOM must change. 
F4: Typed visual builder with presets 
What: React Flow canvas with the 13 modules. An output connects only to an input that 
accepts it, and each node's requires must be connected. Presets (Inventory Starter, 
Approval Chain, Repair Shop) start a site in one click. An inspector edits fields, roles and 
rules through forms, never raw JSON. Example: dragging Approval onto an empty canvas is 
refused: "Approval needs a Request node". Importance: avoids the untyped-canvas and 
developer-only traps. 
F5: Live publish with preview, versions, rollback, history 
What: Draft → validate → preview (live render of the draft, read-only for writes) → publish 
(insert immutable release, flip pointer, write deploy history, one transaction). The runtime 
resolves the active release on every request; schemas are cached by release id. Rollback 
flips the pointer. Example: add Returns, publish, the tab appears. Importance: demo step 3 
and the restart trap. 
Stale saves. Each save carries base_version; a mismatch returns 409 "draft changed, 
reload" with what changed, so nothing is lost. 
Impact-aware publish. Before Publish is enabled, show the diff with counts: "hides field 
price (412 rows hold data, values kept), adds a Returns tab, changes 2 role permissions, 
14 open requests keep running under v5." Destructive changes need 
confirmDestructive. 
Rollback safety. Field removal is a soft-hide (values stay in data, schemas accept known 
plus hidden keys). Module removal disables routes (404); tables and rows stay. Rollback 
reports rows that no longer conform. 


----- PAGE 158 -----
F6: Strict isolation at ORM and database level 
What: Every business table has site_id uuid NOT NULL. scoped(tx, siteId) 
injects the filter (ORM layer). Postgres RLS under a non-bypass app_user, with FORCE 
ROW LEVEL SECURITY, enforces it again (database layer). Site ids come only from the 
signed token. Example: a Central Stores user calling Service Centre gets 403; a forgotten 
WHERE still returns only their rows. Importance: core winning criterion, with a test per 
endpoint. Connecting as a superuser makes RLS silently do nothing. 
F7: Transactional stock 
What: Assignment runs in one BEGIN … COMMIT: lock request, lock inventory rows in 
sorted item-id order, check stock, insert ledger rows, insert assignment, set ASSIGNED. An 
append-only ledger, CHECK (qty_on_hand >= 0) and idempotency keys prevent 
inconsistency and double deduction. Example: 50 requests for 10 units, exactly 10 succeed. 
Importance: "never leaves inventory inconsistent" is pass-or-fail. 
F8: Calculated fields with schema validation 
What: qty * price is parsed by jsep and checked against a whitelist (literals, field names, 
+ - * / %, comparisons, && || !, ternary, ROUND MIN MAX ABS; depth ≤ 8; no 
member access, no eval). The server recomputes every calculated field on every write 
and overwrites client values. A Zod schema compiled from the release validates every body. 
Example: the form shows TOTAL = QTY × PRICE live; qty.constructor is rejected; a 
tampered total is overwritten. 
F9: Role-restricted Excel 
What: ExcelJS export and import driven by config. Each report defines exportRoles, 
importRoles and maskColumns per role. Imports validate every row, run all-or-nothing 
in one transaction, and return row-level errors. Safety: .xlsx only, size and row caps, and 
cells starting with = + - @ are prefixed to block formula injection. Example: admin exports 
with price and total; staff export has those masked; staff import returns 403. 
F10: Seed data and a real offline backup 
What: Three sites, deterministic seed (§9.18), a user per role per site, OPENING ledger 
movements. pg_dump -Fc script, pg_restore --clean --if-exists script tested on 
a clean database, an admin download button, and npm run demo:reset. Importance: a 
listed deliverable. A browser cache alone is not a backup, so we ship both. 
F11: The remaining modules through the framework 
What: Consumption (cannot exceed assigned minus consumed), Returns (USABLE adds 
stock through the ledger when Inventory is wired, DAMAGED does not), PCB Repair 


----- PAGE 159 -----
(six-column kanban) and three analytics charts, all built through the same module 
framework, not hand-built screens.

9.3 Live publish with no restart 


----- PAGE 167 -----
Draft saved with base_version → POST /validate → POST /publish (one 
transaction: insert immutable release, set sites.active_release_id, write 
deploy_history). The runtime resolves site → active release → config per request. 
Compiled schemas are cached by release id. The renderer polls /config/version. 
Rollback flips the pointer only. 
9.4 Graph validator

edge

inventory"

"Role 'manager' is not defined"

"total refers to itself"
