"Authentication is required" 
9.5 Intent layer 
See Signature 3. The endpoint never publishes; it returns a patch plus repairedCount, or 
the cached fallback draft. 
9.6 Release diff and impact 
●​ GET .../releases/:a/diff/:b: changed modules, fields, roles, pages as 
structured items plus plain English. 
●​ GET .../releases/:v/inflight: non-closed requests grouped by 
config_version. 
●​ GET .../releases/:v/config: any release's config for read-only rendering. 
●​ GET .../draft/impact: counts for impact-aware publish (rows holding 
hidden-field data, open requests, role changes). 
9.7 Expression sandbox 
Parse with jsep, walk the tree against the whitelist (literals, field names, + - * / %, 
comparisons, && || !, ternary, ROUND MIN MAX ABS). Reject member access, 
constructors, depth above 8, anything else. Tests: qty.constructor, (()=>1)(). 
9.8 Assignment transaction (row locking) 


----- PAGE 168 -----
Lock the request FOR UPDATE (must be APPROVED) → sort line items by item id and lock 
inventory rows FOR UPDATE in that order → reject if any qty_on_hand < qty → insert 
one ASSIGN movement per line (negative qty, config_version, idempotency key) → 
insert assignment and lines → set ASSIGNED → COMMIT. A unique violation on the 
idempotency key returns the original result and never deducts twice. SQL sketch in §9.17. 
Returns: USABLE inserts a RETURN movement (+qty) if Inventory is wired; DAMAGED 
records the return and changes no stock. 
9.9 Sessions and revocation 
Access tokens expire in 10 minutes; refresh re-reads user_sites. Revoking a user 
increments users.token_version, checked on every request with a 5-second cache. A 
revoked user gets 401 within seconds. 
9.10 Provisioning endpoint 
POST /api/hub/sites/provision calls create_site, publishes v1 in one 
transaction, returns the ordered step log and elapsed time, then runs the isolation probe 
(admin 200, other-site user 403). 
9.11 Excel engine 
Export requires the role in exportRoles and removes maskColumns[role]. Import 
requires importRoles (else 403), validates every row with the compiled schema, runs 
all-or-nothing in one transaction, returns row-level errors, and is recorded in excel_jobs.

revocation

Zod on every body; never trust a body siteId

Parameterised only; ESLint ban on raw pool use

9.14 Database design 
Platform tables: users (bcrypt hash, token_version), sites (slug, 
active_release_id), user_sites (role per site), module_catalog, site_drafts, 


----- PAGE 170 -----
draft_snapshots, site_releases (immutable JSONB), deploy_history (detail 
jsonb, holds provisioning log). 
Business tables (all site_id NOT NULL): inventory_items, stock_movements 
(append-only), requests, approvals, assignments, assignment_lines, 
consumption_logs, item_returns, pcb_repairs, excel_jobs, sync_actions 
(UNIQUE (site_id, idempotency_key)), audit_log (append-only). 
Custom fields. Typed core columns (site_id, status, qty, config_version, 
status_changed_at) plus a data jsonb column. Two sites with different fields share 
one table. 
Module tables exist before publish. The base migration creates all 13 modules' tables. 
Publish only changes which routes are enabled; a route for a module not in the active 
release returns 404. 
The ledger always matches stock. An AFTER INSERT trigger on stock_movements 
applies the quantity to qty_on_hand; nothing else writes it. Seed data creates OPENING 
movements. Test: SUM(movements) = qty_on_hand. 
Integrity extras: composite FKs (id, site_id) make cross-site references impossible; 
triggers set status_changed_at and block UPDATE/DELETE on ledger and audit; 
config_version on requests, movements and audit rows; indexes (site_id, 
status), (site_id, created_at), (site_id, item_id); paginated lists so RLS 
stays fast. 
Pre-demo check (daily and before every run): 
SELECT current_user, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user; 
-- must be rolbypassrls = false

9.16 RLS SQL 
CREATE ROLE app_user LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS; 
 
CREATE FUNCTION app_site_ids() RETURNS uuid[] LANGUAGE sql STABLE AS $$ 
  SELECT COALESCE( 
    string_to_array(NULLIF(current_setting('app.site_ids', true), ''), ',')::uuid[], 
    ARRAY[]::uuid[]) 
$$; 
 
ALTER TABLE requests ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY; 
ALTER TABLE requests FORCE  ROW LEVEL SECURITY; 
CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation ON requests 
  USING      (site_id = ANY (app_site_ids())) 
  WITH CHECK (site_id = ANY (app_site_ids())); 
-- repeated for every tenant table; unset setting => zero rows (fails closed) 
 
-- per request, inside withTenant: 
-- BEGIN; SELECT set_config('app.site_ids', $1, true); …queries…; COMMIT; 
 
SET LOCAL cannot take bind parameters, and a plain SET leaks one request's sites into the 
next on a pooled connection. 
9.17 Assignment transaction (sketch) 
BEGIN; 
SELECT set_config('app.site_ids', $site, true); 


----- PAGE 172 -----
SELECT * FROM requests WHERE id =

9.18 Deterministic seed spec 
So the proof numbers are real, the seed is fixed: 120 requests in total, 34 in Service 
Centre and 86 in Distribution (Central Stores holds inventory only). The Trust Console 
runs a no-WHERE SELECT and the dot grid shows 120 dots with the logged-in site's rows lit. 
The UI always queries these counts; it never hardcodes them. Stock for each item starts as 
an OPENING movement. Include ≥ 100 rows across inventory, requests, repairs, returns and 
consumption, plus one user per role per site.

12. Business impact (measured, not claimed) 
●​ Time to provision a new site: __ seconds, 0 server restarts. 
●​ Site-specific code lines: 0 (CI check). 
●​ Reuse: 13 modules × 3 sites, 0 forks. 
●​ Isolation: N / N endpoints tested, no leaks. 
●​ Concurrency: 0 oversells in 50 parallel requests. 
Who benefits: warehouses, service centres, manufacturers and distributors with several 
units and changing workflows. A process owner changes a form or approval chain without a 
developer, a bad release is rewound in one drag, and admins get a combined view. For 
Electrolyte: onboarding a new client site becomes configuration, not a new project. Do not 
claim cost savings you have not measured. 
 
13. Judge Q&A 
●​ "Isn't this just different colours?" No. Three shells and four layouts are chosen by 
config and the DOM changes. 
●​ "What if the AI is wrong?" It only drafts a patch. The compiler can reject it, errors 
feed back for repair, a human reviews the diff, and publishing is manual. 
●​ "How is isolation enforced?" In the ORM wrapper and in Postgres RLS under a 
role that cannot bypass it. Attack it in the Trust Console. 
●​ "Can a bad edit break another site?" Config and code are separate, releases are 
immutable, isolation is tested per endpoint, and CI forbids site slugs in source. 
●​ "What happens to open requests on rollback?" They keep the config version they 
started under. Rollback moves a pointer and never touches data. Removed fields are 
hidden, not deleted. 
●​ "Two admins edit at once?" The second save gets a 409 and loses nothing. 
●​ "Can offline corrupt stock?" No. Offline can only read, or ask; the server decides, 
with idempotency keys. 


----- PAGE 176 -----
●​ "How is this different from Retool or Odoo?" Retool builds single CRUD apps; 
Odoo is a fixed suite. OpsHub assembles different isolated sites from typed modules 
and releases them live. 
●​ "Why these extras if scope is frozen?" Each one is a new experience of a required 
objective, not a new module. 
●​ "How do you know your numbers?" They are queried live from the database; 
nothing on the Trust Console is hardcoded.

1. The problem and what the organizer is judging 
1.1 The problem 
A company runs three units: Central Stores (inventory), Service Centre (requests, 
approvals, repairs, returns) and Distribution (material requests, assignments, consumption). 
All need the same basic features but want different fields, rules and layouts, so each gets its 
own codebase. 3 units × 12 modules = 36 implementations where 12 shared ones would do. 
Consequences: separate logins, no combined admin view, process owners who need a 
developer to change a form or approval chain, shared-code edits that break other sites, and 
releases with no preview or rollback. 
1.2 What the organizer expects 
One codebase running N completely different sites in three layers: 
●​ Hub: single app shell, global JWT + bcrypt login, RBAC, user management, site 
switcher, theme engine, module catalogue. 
●​ Site: { siteId, slug, theme, modules, pages, roles }. 
●​ Module and node: 13 approved modules as typed nodes with defined inputs and 
outputs. 
1.3 The traps that disqualify 
Trap

Scope creep 
The 13 modules are frozen. Depth beats breadth 
Colour-palette trap 
Sites must differ in layout, modules and workflow, not just colours 


----- PAGE 183 -----
Server restart trap 
Publishing needs no code change and no restart

Untyped canvas

1.4 The winning criteria 
Strict site_id isolation at ORM and database level with a test for every endpoint · ACID stock 
with row locking · one universal renderer over JSONB · calculated fields with schema 
validation · a rigid four-step demo · seeded data and an offline backup.

2. Our solution in one page 
OpsHub is a configuration brain with a universal body. 
An admin designs a site by voice, by typing, or on a typed canvas. The Brain validates the 
design, compiles it, and saves it as an immutable versioned release. One universal renderer 
turns the release into the interface. One generic API runs the workflows. PostgreSQL keeps 
every site's data isolated. 
The promise: a new site is a new configuration. It never needs new code. 
The pitch line: "Three units used to mean 36 implementations. We built 13 modules once, 
and configuration does the rest." 
What OpsHub is famous for: 
The platform where you speak a business into existence, scrub through its history 
like a video, keep it running in a warehouse dead zone, and invite you to try to 
break it. 
Product people 
remember 
One-sentence hook 
OpsHub's equivalent 
ResQLink 
Works with no 
internet 
Dead-Zone Lite: every demo page still 
renders offline 
Kisaan Vaani 
Talk to an AI in your 
language 
Bolo & Build: describe a process in Hindi, 
Marathi or English 
Instagram Reels 
Scrub, swipe, watch 
Release Rewind: drag a slider, the live site 
morphs 
The moat: the release is an immutable, versioned unit. A site is a pointer to a release. 
Transactions are pinned to the config version they ran under. Rollback moves one pointer and 
never rewrites business data. 
Honest safety claim: one site's edit cannot break another because of config-versus-code 
separation, immutable releases, isolation tests, and a CI check that no site slug appears in 
source. We do not claim a merge engine solves this. 


----- PAGE 185 -----
Positioning: OpsHub is to internal operations software what Shopify is to online stores, with 
Vercel-style preview, publish and rollback.

3. Architecture 
Voice / typed prompt ──► Intent layer (LLM returns a config PATCH only) 
                                   │ 
 ┌──────────────── BRAIN (config engine) ────────────────┐ 
 │ Module registry → Typed graph validator (subtype-aware)│ 
 │ → Compiler (Zod + jsep) → Immutable release (JSONB) →  │ 
 │ Active-release pointer · Release diff · Impact · Rollback│ 
 
└───────────┬──────────────────────────┬─────────────
───┘ 
             ▼                          ▼ 
   Universal renderer            Generic runtime API 
   (also renders any past        /api/:siteId/:resource 
    release read-only) 
             └──────────┬───────────────┘ 
                        ▼

                        ▲

----- PAGE 186 -----
 
Layer 
Components

Offline cache, proof panels, SLA monitor, cross-site search 
Stack: React 18, Vite, TypeScript, Tailwind, React Router, Zustand + IndexedDB 
persistence, Axios, React Flow, Recharts, React Hook Form, Zod, jsep, vite-plugin-pwa / 
Node, Express, JWT, bcrypt, ExcelJS, Drizzle ORM wrapped in scoped(tx, siteId) / 
PostgreSQL 16 in Docker. Tests: Vitest + Supertest. CI: GitHub Actions. 
Postgres or Supabase? Docker Postgres is primary: the demo must work offline and the PS 
needs our own JWT and bcrypt login. The schema is Supabase-compatible, but we always 
connect as app_user, because default roles can bypass RLS. 
Request pipeline: JWT verify (10 min + token_version) → requireSite (URL site must be in 
token siteIds, else 403) → requireRole (from release config) → idempotency check (writes) 
→ validateBody (Zod compiled from release) → withTenant (BEGIN, set_config, query 
through scoped(), COMMIT) → audit log. An ESLint rule bans raw pool.query outside db/. 
 
4. The 13 modules (the building blocks) 
Every module is a typed node. The validator uses requires, input types and output types to 
reject invalid designs. 
Type tree: Record → StockItem, RequestRecord (→ ApprovedRequest), Assignment, 
ConsumptionRecord, ReturnRecord, RepairRecord. Standalone: Session, Role, User, Page, 
Chart, File. An input accepting RequestRecord also accepts ApprovedRequest, never the 
reverse. "Any record" means Record. 
A node's requires is satisfied only when a compatible edge actually connects. Modules 1 
and 4 are platform nodes: auto-present and locked in every site.

Validator rules: every requires has a connected compatible edge · edge types are 
subtype-aware · no cycles except declared workflow loops · every page role exists · formulas 
parse, reference real fields, have no cycles · platform nodes cannot be deleted. 
Negative tests: Approval without Request, Chart into Request, Material Assignment without 
Inventory, Assignment fed by a plain RequestRecord, and Inventory → Returns are all 
rejected. Approval → PCB Repair and Request → Returns are accepted. 
 
5. Signature features (S+): why people will remember us 
Signature 1: Release Rewind (Time Slider) 
What judges see. On the Releases page, a slider with one tick per version (v1 … v5). The 
presenter drags back. The live site morphs: the Returns tab disappears, a field vanishes, the 
layout shifts. Drag forward and it returns. A plain-English report appears: "v5 → v4: removes 


----- PAGE 189 -----
1 module and 2 fields. 14 open requests keep running under v5." A Rollback button commits 
the choice. 
How it works. 
●​ Releases are immutable JSONB, so the renderer can draw any release read-only. The 
slider swaps the config the preview renders. 
●​ A diff function summarises changes in words. The same function powers 
impact-aware publish, so it is almost free. 
●​ The in-flight panel counts open requests per config_version. 
●​ Rollback flips the active-release pointer and never touches business data. 
Importance. It turns the dullest required objective (preview, versions, rollback, history) into 
the most visual moment of the demo, and answers the organizer's complaint directly: "with no 
preview or rollback, every release is risky." 
Example line. "It's gone. It's back. Nothing in the database changed." 
Risk. Running in-flight requests under their pinned release is a stretch goal. MVP shows the 
count and the pinned version. 
Maps to: preview, versioned releases, rollback, deploy history. Cost: ~4 h. Cut rule: never 
cut (fallback: diff without the morph animation). 
Signature 2: Trust Console ("Try to break it") 
What judges see. A Hub page where the judge is invited to attack, in three panels: 
1.​ Isolation. A forged site id or tampered token gets 403 from Express. Below it, a 
query with no WHERE clause in code returns a dot grid of the seeded rows with 
only the user's lit (for example 34 of 120). That is Postgres filtering, not the app. 
Counts are queried live. A psql check with no setting returns 0 rows. 
2.​ Concurrency. One button fires 50 parallel assignments for 10 units: 10 succeed, 40 
rejected, stock 0, ledger balanced. 
3.​ Why was I blocked? Every rejection has a drawer with its decision trace: rule, inputs, 
config version. 
How it works. Demo-mode-only endpoints (DEMO_MODE=true), app_user with RLS, FOR 
UPDATE row locks, idempotency keys, the append-only audit log. Show the 403 and the 
filtering as two separate proofs. 
Importance. Isolation and transactional integrity are the PS's core winning criteria, and most 
teams will only claim them. We make them tangible. Inviting an attack is a confidence signal 
almost no team sends. 
Risk. Check rolbypassrls = false before every demo, or the proof is fake. 


----- PAGE 190 -----
Maps to: per-endpoint isolation tests and transactional integrity. Cost: ~5 h. Cut rule: never 
cut. 
Signature 3: Bolo & Build (describe a process, in your language) 
What judges see. The presenter types, or taps the mic and says: "Distribution mein returns 
jodo. Kharab saman inspection ke baad hi wapas stock mein aaye." On the canvas, nodes 
appear one by one: Request → Returns, with an inspection field and a USABLE/DAMAGED 
rule. The validator ticks each green. If the AI erred, a chip says "fixed 1 issue automatically". 
The presenter reviews the diff and presses Publish. English fallback: "Add Returns to 
Distribution. Damaged items must not go back into stock." 
How it works. 
●​ POST /api/hub/copilot/patch takes {siteSlug, text, lang} plus the module catalogue 
and returns a config patch constrained by a JSON schema (known module keys, field 
types and edges only). 
●​ The compiler validates. Errors go back to the model for up to three repair rounds. 
repairedCount drives the chip. 
●​ The AI drafts. The compiler checks. A human publishes. If the API fails, a cached 
fallbackDraft.json loads. 
●​ Voice uses the Web Speech API (hi-IN, mr-IN, en-IN). The typed box uses the same 
path. 
Example. "approval chain with two approvers above ₹25,000" → Approval with total > 
25000 → 2 approvals, evaluated by the same jsep sandbox. 
Importance. The strongest answer to "a builder only developers can use misses the point". It 
makes live-publish memorable, and Hindi/Marathi input in Mumbai is a local-relevance 
moment. 
Risks. Speech needs internet and a quiet room: use a headset, pre-test the exact sentences, 
keep the typed box and cached draft ready. 
Maps to: non-developer builder objective. Cost: ~4 h typed, +2 h voice. Cut rule: cut voice 
first; the typed co-pilot stays. 
Signature 4: Dead-Zone Lite (works with no network) 
What judges see. On a real phone with the PWA installed, the presenter stops the server or 
switches on airplane mode. An "Offline: viewing cached data" banner appears and every 
demo page still renders from cache. Write buttons are disabled with a tooltip. 
How it works. 


----- PAGE 191 -----
●​ Every demo page is pre-warmed into the per-user IndexedDB cache on load, so a 
never-opened tab is not blank. 
●​ /api/health pings every 5 s; a request that fails with no response counts as offline. 
●​ vite-plugin-pwa precaches the app shell. The cache is cleared on logout. 
Stretch. A write queue for request.create and consumption.log only, replayed through POST 
/api/:siteId/sync with idempotency keys. Rule: offline can ask, only the server can decide, 
so approve and assign stay online-only. QR scan on top. 
Risks. Camera and PWA install need HTTPS (localhost exempt): test a tunnel or local 
certificate on day one. Dry-run the stop-and-restart before the demo. 
Maps to: the offline backup deliverable and Consumption/Return inputs. Cost: ~3 h (lite). 
Cut rule: first to cut beyond the banner and cached reads; the real pg_dump backup stays 
regardless. 
Signature 5: One-Save Provisioning 
What judges see. Create a site from a preset and save once. A live log ticks through real 
steps: site registered, draft seeded, config validated, release v1 published, roles assigned, 
endpoints live, isolation verified. A timer shows the total. The last step probes the new site: 
200 for its admin, 403 for another site's user. 
How it works. One transaction runs create_site (a SECURITY DEFINER function with a 
hub-admin check) and publishes v1. The admin's token is re-issued so siteIds includes the 
new site. "Endpoints live" is honest: the generic router resolves routes from config, so 
nothing is registered or restarted. 
Importance. The PS says one builder puts together completely different sites, each with its 
own isolated backend, in a single save. This is that sentence made visible. 
Maps to: the single-save objective. Cost: ~2.5 h. Cut rule: cut second; use it as a 20-second 
Q&A clip.

6. Foundations (MVP): the baseline that must be flawless 
Nobody remembers these, but missing any one loses points or the whole event. 
F1: One login, many sites, a Hub admin view 
What: One login issues a JWT with siteIds and per-site roles. A site switcher loads each site 
with its own theme, pages and data. Hub KPIs run in one withTenant over all of the admin's 
sites. Example: log in once, see all three units, enter Service Centre, no second login. 
Importance: removes "separate logins, no combined view"; demo step 1. 
F2: One universal renderer 
What: One React renderer reads the site's JSONB config and produces shell, layout, forms 
and tables. No site-specific React files. Example: Central Stores draws a sidebar and stock 
table; Service Centre draws a top bar and a kanban; same renderer. Importance: the heart of 
the PS. CI fails if a site slug appears in source. 
F3: Layout-changing sites 
What: Config selects one of three shells (sidebar, topbar-cards, workbench) and one of four 
layouts (table, kanban, split, dashboard); forms are shared; theme tokens are the smallest 
difference. Example: Distribution is a split pane with a stock availability meter; Central 
Stores is a plain table. Importance: the organizer says swapping CSS variables is not 
enough; the DOM must change. 
F4: Typed visual builder with presets 
What: React Flow canvas with the 13 modules. An output connects only to an input that 
accepts it, and each node's requires must be connected. Presets (Inventory Starter, Approval 
Chain, Repair Shop) start a site in one click. An inspector edits fields, roles and rules through 
forms, never raw JSON. Example: dragging Approval onto an empty canvas is refused: 
"Approval needs a Request node". Importance: avoids the untyped-canvas and 
developer-only traps. 
F5: Live publish with preview, versions, rollback, history 


----- PAGE 193 -----
What: Draft → validate → preview (live render of the draft, read-only for writes) → 
publish (insert immutable release, flip pointer, write deploy history, one transaction). The 
runtime resolves the active release on every request; schemas are cached by release id. 
Rollback flips the pointer. Example: add Returns, publish, the tab appears. Importance: 
demo step 3 and the restart trap. 
Stale saves. Each save carries base_version; a mismatch returns 409 "draft changed, 
reload" with what changed, so nothing is lost. 
Impact-aware publish. Before Publish is enabled, show the diff with counts: "hides field 
price (412 rows hold data, values kept), adds a Returns tab, changes 2 role permissions, 14 
open requests keep running under v5." Destructive changes need confirmDestructive. 
Rollback safety. Field removal is a soft-hide (values stay in data, schemas accept known 
plus hidden keys). Module removal disables routes (404); tables and rows stay. Rollback 
reports rows that no longer conform. 
F6: Strict isolation at ORM and database level 
What: Every business table has site_id uuid NOT NULL. scoped(tx, siteId) injects the filter 
(ORM layer). Postgres RLS under a non-bypass app_user, with FORCE ROW LEVEL 
SECURITY, enforces it again (database layer). Site ids come only from the signed token. 
Example: a Central Stores user calling Service Centre gets 403; a forgotten WHERE still 
returns only their rows. Importance: core winning criterion, with a test per endpoint. 
Connecting as a superuser makes RLS silently do nothing. 
F7: Transactional stock 
What: Assignment runs in one BEGIN … COMMIT: lock request, lock inventory rows in 
sorted item-id order, check stock, insert ledger rows, insert assignment, set ASSIGNED. An 
append-only ledger, CHECK (qty_on_hand >= 0) and idempotency keys prevent 
inconsistency and double deduction. Example: 50 requests for 10 units, exactly 10 succeed. 
Importance: "never leaves inventory inconsistent" is pass-or-fail. 
F8: Calculated fields with schema validation 
What: qty * price is parsed by jsep and checked against a whitelist (literals, field names, + - 
* / %, comparisons, && || !, ternary, ROUND MIN MAX ABS; depth ≤ 8; no member 
access, no eval). The server recomputes every calculated field on every write and 
overwrites client values. A Zod schema compiled from the release validates every body. 
Example: the form shows TOTAL = QTY × PRICE live; qty.constructor is rejected; a 
tampered total is overwritten. 
F9: Role-restricted Excel 


----- PAGE 194 -----
What: ExcelJS export and import driven by config. Each report defines exportRoles, 
importRoles and maskColumns per role. Imports validate every row, run all-or-nothing in 
one transaction, and return row-level errors. Safety: .xlsx only, size and row caps, and cells 
starting with = + - @ are prefixed to block formula injection. Example: admin exports with 
price and total; staff export has those masked; staff import returns 403. 
F10: Seed data and a real offline backup 
What: Three sites, deterministic seed (§9.18), a user per role per site, OPENING ledger 
movements. pg_dump -Fc script, pg_restore --clean --if-exists script tested on a clean 
database, an admin download button, and npm run demo:reset. Importance: a listed 
deliverable. A browser cache alone is not a backup, so we ship both. 
F11: The remaining modules through the framework 
What: Consumption (cannot exceed assigned minus consumed), Returns (USABLE adds 
stock through the ledger when Inventory is wired, DAMAGED does not), PCB Repair 
(six-column kanban) and three analytics charts, all built through the same module framework, 
not hand-built screens.

9.3 Live publish with no restart 
Draft saved with base_version → POST /validate → POST /publish (one transaction: insert 
immutable release, set sites.active_release_id, write deploy_history). The runtime resolves 
site → active release → config per request. Compiled schemas are cached by release id. The 
renderer polls /config/version. Rollback flips the pointer only. 
9.4 Graph validator 
Rule 
Example message 
Every requires has a connected compatible edge 
"Approval needs a Request node" 
Output type accepted by target input 
(subtype-aware) 
"Chart cannot feed Request" 
No cycles except declared workflow loops 
"Cycle: inventory → request → 
inventory" 


----- PAGE 203 -----
Every page role exists 
"Role 'manager' is not defined" 
Formulas parse, reference real fields, no cycles 
"total refers to itself" 
Platform nodes cannot be deleted 
"Authentication is required" 
9.5 Intent layer 
See Signature 3. The endpoint never publishes; it returns a patch plus repairedCount, or the 
cached fallback draft. 
9.6 Release diff and impact 
●​ GET .../releases/:a/diff/:b: changed modules, fields, roles, pages as structured items 
plus plain English. 
●​ GET .../releases/:v/inflight: non-closed requests grouped by config_version. 
●​ GET .../releases/:v/config: any release's config for read-only rendering. 
●​ GET .../draft/impact: counts for impact-aware publish (rows holding hidden-field 
data, open requests, role changes). 
9.7 Expression sandbox 
Parse with jsep, walk the tree against the whitelist (literals, field names, + - * / %, 
comparisons, && || !, ternary, ROUND MIN MAX ABS). Reject member access, 
constructors, depth above 8, anything else. Tests: qty.constructor, (()=>1)(). 
9.8 Assignment transaction (row locking) 
Lock the request FOR UPDATE (must be APPROVED) → sort line items by item id and 
lock inventory rows FOR UPDATE in that order → reject if any qty_on_hand < qty → insert 
one ASSIGN movement per line (negative qty, config_version, idempotency key) → insert 
assignment and lines → set ASSIGNED → COMMIT. A unique violation on the 
idempotency key returns the original result and never deducts twice. SQL sketch in §9.17. 
Returns: USABLE inserts a RETURN movement (+qty) if Inventory is wired; DAMAGED 
records the return and changes no stock. 
9.9 Sessions and revocation 
Access tokens expire in 10 minutes; refresh re-reads user_sites. Revoking a user increments 
users.token_version, checked on every request with a 5-second cache. A revoked user gets 
401 within seconds. 
9.10 Provisioning endpoint 


----- PAGE 204 -----
POST /api/hub/sites/provision calls create_site, publishes v1 in one transaction, returns the 
ordered step log and elapsed time, then runs the isolation probe (admin 200, other-site user 
403). 
9.11 Excel engine 
Export requires the role in exportRoles and removes maskColumns[role]. Import requires 
importRoles (else 403), validates every row with the compiled schema, runs all-or-nothing in 
one transaction, returns row-level errors, and is recorded in excel_jobs.

9.14 Database design 
Platform tables: users (bcrypt hash, token_version), sites (slug, active_release_id), 
user_sites (role per site), module_catalog, site_drafts, draft_snapshots, site_releases 
(immutable JSONB), deploy_history (detail jsonb, holds provisioning log). 
Business tables (all site_id NOT NULL): inventory_items, stock_movements 
(append-only), requests, approvals, assignments, assignment_lines, consumption_logs, 
item_returns, pcb_repairs, excel_jobs, sync_actions (UNIQUE (site_id, idempotency_key)), 
audit_log (append-only). 
Custom fields. Typed core columns (site_id, status, qty, config_version, status_changed_at) 
plus a data jsonb column. Two sites with different fields share one table. 
Module tables exist before publish. The base migration creates all 13 modules' tables. 
Publish only changes which routes are enabled; a route for a module not in the active release 
returns 404. 
The ledger always matches stock. An AFTER INSERT trigger on stock_movements applies 
the quantity to qty_on_hand; nothing else writes it. Seed data creates OPENING movements. 
Test: SUM(movements) = qty_on_hand. 
Integrity extras: composite FKs (id, site_id) make cross-site references impossible; triggers 
set status_changed_at and block UPDATE/DELETE on ledger and audit; config_version on 
requests, movements and audit rows; indexes (site_id, status), (site_id, created_at), (site_id, 
item_id); paginated lists so RLS stays fast. 


----- PAGE 206 -----
Pre-demo check (daily and before every run): 
SELECT current_user, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user; 
-- must be rolbypassrls = false

9.16 RLS SQL 
CREATE ROLE app_user LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS; 


----- PAGE 207 -----
 
CREATE FUNCTION app_site_ids() RETURNS uuid[] LANGUAGE sql STABLE AS $$ 
  SELECT COALESCE( 
    string_to_array(NULLIF(current_setting('app.site_ids', true), ''), ',')::uuid[], 
    ARRAY[]::uuid[]) 
$$; 
 
ALTER TABLE requests ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY; 
ALTER TABLE requests FORCE  ROW LEVEL SECURITY; 
CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation ON requests 
  USING      (site_id = ANY (app_site_ids())) 
  WITH CHECK (site_id = ANY (app_site_ids())); 
-- repeated for every tenant table; unset setting => zero rows (fails closed) 
 
-- per request, inside withTenant: 
-- BEGIN; SELECT set_config('app.site_ids', $1, true); …queries…; COMMIT; 
 
SET LOCAL cannot take bind parameters, and a plain SET leaks one request's sites into the 
next on a pooled connection. 
9.17 Assignment transaction (sketch) 
BEGIN; 
SELECT set_config('app.site_ids', $site, true); 
SELECT * FROM requests WHERE id = 
𝑟𝑒𝑞𝐹𝑂𝑅𝑈𝑃𝐷𝐴𝑇𝐸; −−𝑚𝑢𝑠𝑡𝑏𝑒𝐴𝑃𝑃𝑅𝑂𝑉𝐸𝐷𝑆𝐸𝐿𝐸𝐶𝑇𝑖𝑑, 𝑞𝑡𝑦𝑜𝑛ℎ𝑎𝑛𝑑𝐹𝑅𝑂𝑀𝑖𝑛𝑣𝑒𝑛𝑡𝑜𝑟𝑦𝑖𝑡𝑒𝑚𝑠𝑊𝐻𝐸𝑅𝐸𝑖𝑑= 𝐴
item_ids) ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE;              -- fixed lock order, no deadlock 
-- app: reject if any qty_on_hand < requested qty 
INSERT INTO stock_movements (site_id, item_id, qty, kind, config_version, 
idempotency_key) 
  VALUES (…, -$qty, 'ASSIGN', $ver, $key);                        -- per line; unique key => replay 
returns original 
INSERT INTO assignments …; INSERT INTO assignment_lines …; 
UPDATE requests SET status = 'ASSIGNED' WHERE id = $req; 
COMMIT; 
 
9.18 Deterministic seed spec 
So the proof numbers are real, the seed is fixed: 120 requests in total, 34 in Service Centre 
and 86 in Distribution (Central Stores holds inventory only). The Trust Console runs a 
no-WHERE SELECT and the dot grid shows 120 dots with the logged-in site's rows lit. The 
UI always queries these counts; it never hardcodes them. Stock for each item starts as an 


----- PAGE 208 -----
OPENING movement. Include ≥ 100 rows across inventory, requests, repairs, returns and 
consumption, plus one user per role per site.

12. Business impact (measured, not claimed) 
●​ Time to provision a new site: __ seconds, 0 server restarts. 
●​ Site-specific code lines: 0 (CI check). 
●​ Reuse: 13 modules × 3 sites, 0 forks. 
●​ Isolation: N / N endpoints tested, no leaks. 
●​ Concurrency: 0 oversells in 50 parallel requests. 
Who benefits: warehouses, service centres, manufacturers and distributors with several units 
and changing workflows. A process owner changes a form or approval chain without a 
developer, a bad release is rewound in one drag, and admins get a combined view. For 
Electrolyte: onboarding a new client site becomes configuration, not a new project. Do not 
claim cost savings you have not measured. 
 
13. Judge Q&A 
●​ "Isn't this just different colours?" No. Three shells and four layouts are chosen by 
config and the DOM changes. 
●​ "What if the AI is wrong?" It only drafts a patch. The compiler can reject it, errors 
feed back for repair, a human reviews the diff, and publishing is manual. 
●​ "How is isolation enforced?" In the ORM wrapper and in Postgres RLS under a role 
that cannot bypass it. Attack it in the Trust Console. 
●​ "Can a bad edit break another site?" Config and code are separate, releases are 
immutable, isolation is tested per endpoint, and CI forbids site slugs in source. 
●​ "What happens to open requests on rollback?" They keep the config version they 
started under. Rollback moves a pointer and never touches data. Removed fields are 
hidden, not deleted. 
●​ "Two admins edit at once?" The second save gets a 409 and loses nothing. 
●​ "Can offline corrupt stock?" No. Offline can only read, or ask; the server decides, 
with idempotency keys. 
●​ "How is this different from Retool or Odoo?" Retool builds single CRUD apps; 
Odoo is a fixed suite. OpsHub assembles different isolated sites from typed modules 
and releases them live. 
●​ "Why these extras if scope is frozen?" Each one is a new experience of a required 
objective, not a new module. 
●​ "How do you know your numbers?" They are queried live from the database; 
nothing on the Trust Console is hardcoded.

----- PAGE 217 -----
Based on the comprehensive OpsHub document and your specific constraints, here is a 
highly optimized division of labor for a 4-person team in a 24-hour hackathon. 
This structure ensures that Frontend and Backend are developed in parallel, Features are 
strictly isolated to avoid merge conflicts, Automation is handled properly, and Integration is 
exclusively managed by a single member to prevent duplicate wiring and chaotic state 
management. 
It also includes a structured timeline to guarantee buffer time for modifications, bug fixes, 
and the mandatory 5x demo rehearsal. 
👤 Member 1: The Integrator & DevOps Lead (The "Glue") 
Role: Sole owner of Integration, Automation, and DevOps. This member does not build the 
isolated UI components or the core database logic; instead, they wire everything together, 
manage the state, and automate the pipelines. 
●​ Integration (Exclusive Owner): 
○​ Stitches the Frontend (React/Zustand) to the Backend (Express/Drizzle). 
○​ Writes all API call layers (Axios instances, error interceptors). 
○​ Passes the JWT tokens, handles the 10-minute expiry/refresh flow, and 
manages the global state (site switcher). 
○​ Wires the JSON config from the Builder (Member 3) to the Universal 
Renderer (Member 4). 
●​ Automation: 
○​ Writes the CI/CD pipeline (GitHub Actions) for the "slug grep" check and test 
runner. 
○​ Creates the 1-command Docker Compose setup. 
○​ Writes and tests the npm run demo:reset script (critical for rehearsing the 
demo). 
●​ Backend Support: 
○​ Sets up the Express server shell, middleware (rate limiting, auth check, body 
validation), and idempotency keys. 
●​ Feature Ownership: One-Save Provisioning (S5) – orchestrating the backend site 
creation function with the frontend real-time log. 
👤 Member 2: The Data & Security Architect (Backend Heavy) 
Role: The core backend brain. Handles everything related to the database, isolation, 
transactions, and security. 
●​ Backend Coding: 
○​ Sets up Postgres 16, Drizzle ORM, and the base migrations. 
○​ Implements the crucial Row Level Security (RLS) policies (app_user, 
NOBYPASSRLS). 
○​ Writes the withTenant / scoped(tx) wrappers to ensure site_id is 
injected into every query. 


----- PAGE 218 -----
●​ Feature Ownership: 
○​ Transactional Stock (M7): Writes the complex, deadlock-free inventory 
assignment logic (sorting lines by item ID, locking rows FOR UPDATE). 
○​ Trust Console (S2): Builds the isolation proofs, the 50-concurrent-requests 
endpoint, and the dot-grid query. 
○​ Excel Engine (M9 & M13): Builds the secure import/export endpoints, 
enforcing limits and preventing formula injection. 
●​ Testing: Writes the backend isolation matrix, pool leak tests, and idempotency tests. 
👤 Member 3: The Brain & Builder (Frontend Heavy) 
Role: The visual architect. Builds the complex interactive tools used by the Hub Admin to 
design and configure sites. 
●​ Frontend Coding: 
○​ Builds the Hub UI pages (/hub, /hub/sites, /hub/builder). 
○​ Implements the Typed Canvas using React Flow (node palette, typed edges, 
subtype validation). 
○​ Sets up the Theme Engine (CSS variables mapping from config). 
●​ Feature Ownership: 
○​ OpsHub Compiler: Writes the Zod + jsep logic that turns the visual graph 
into validated JSON and parses mathematical formulas securely. 
○​ Bolo & Build (S3): Integrates the LLM patch generation, the voice API (Web 
Speech API for Hindi/Marathi/English), and the fallback JSON. 
○​ Release Rewind (S1): Builds the time slider, the plain-English diff generator, 
and the "Impact-aware publish" modal. 
●​ Testing: Writes the graph validation tests (ensuring cyclical loops or bad connections 
fail) and expression tampering tests. 
👤 Member 4: The Runtime Specialist (Fullstack Business Logic) 
Role: The engine driver. Takes the JSON configuration outputted by Member 3 and turns it 
into working business pages. 
●​ Frontend & Backend Coding: 
○​ Builds the Universal Renderer that reads the active release and draws the 
layouts (Table, Kanban, Split, Dashboard). 
○​ Builds the 13 frozen modules (Requests, Approvals, Assignments, 
Consumption, Returns, PCB Repair). 
●​ Feature Ownership: 
○​ Dead-Zone Lite (S4): Configures vite-plugin-pwa, the IndexedDB 
caching layer, and the offline banner. 
○​ Dynamic UI Elements: Builds the Aging SLA rings, the Ctrl+K cross-site 
search palette, and Recharts analytics dashboards. 
●​ Testing: Writes the module routing tests (ensuring disabled modules return 404/hide 
from UI) and offline pre-warming tests. 


----- PAGE 219 -----
⏱️ Timeline & Synchronization Strategy (Ensuring Modification Time) 
To ensure top-quality code and leave enough time for bug fixing, integration smoothing, and 
demo practice, follow this strict timeline: 
Phase 1: Foundations & Mocks (Hours 0 - 8) — The "Gate" 
●​ Member 1: Setup Docker, CI/CD, Express routing, and mocked API responses. 
●​ Member 2: DB schema, RLS, withTenant wrapper, Inventory logic. 
●​ Member 3: React Flow canvas basic UI, Hub layouts. 
●​ Member 4: Universal Renderer shell, dummy JSON configs to render basic tables. 
●​ Checkpoint (Hour 8): Member 1 connects login -> Hub -> Site Switcher. Database 
RLS must be proven to work. 
Phase 2: Core Workflows & Visuals (Hours 8 - 16) 
●​ Member 1: Connects the React Flow Builder (FE) to the Postgres Config tables (BE). 
Implements API states using Zustand. 
●​ Member 2: Trust Console endpoints, Excel endpoints, Assignment locking 
transactions. 
●​ Member 3: Jsep validation, Release Rewind slider UI, Bolo & Build LLM connection. 
●​ Member 4: Dynamic forms working, SLA rings, Kanban drag-and-drop. 
Phase 3: Signature Features & Feature Freeze (Hours 16 - 20) 
●​ Member 1: One-Save provisioning flow integrated. Writes the demo:reset script. 
●​ Member 2: Finishes backend testing (pool leaks, concurrency). Reviews DB 
performance. 
●​ Member 3: Finishes diff generator. Polishes UI/UX (Themes/Colours). 
●​ Member 4: Offline Dead-Zone PWA testing, Analytics charts wired up. 
●​ Checkpoint (Hour 20): FEATURE FREEZE. No new code. 
Phase 4: Modification, Polish, and Demo (Hours 20 - 24) — The Buffer 
●​ Hours 20 - 21: Modification Window. Member 1 reviews all integration points. The 
team fixes any broken state updates, visual bugs, or failing CI tests. 
●​ Hours 21 - 22: Run the exact rigid 4-step demo script (as per document §18) slowly. 
Identify bottlenecks. 
●​ Hours 22 - 24: Rehearse the 3-minute demo 5 times with a stopwatch. Record the 
fallback video. Run the final rolbypassrls check to ensure the isolation proof is 
real. 
Why this division guarantees success: 
1.​ Zero Merge Conflicts on Core Logic: Member 2 owns the DB, Member 3 owns the 
Builder, Member 4 owns the Renderer. They work in completely separate folders 
(/db, /builder-ui, /runtime-ui). 


----- PAGE 220 -----
2.​ Integration is Bottleneck-Free: Because Member 1 is doing nothing but integration 
and DevOps, developers 2, 3, and 4 don't waste time figuring out how to pass state 
between each other's components. They just write clean isolated code, and Member 
1 plugs it in. 
3.​ Built-in Quality Assurance: By freezing features at Hour 20, you reserve a massive 
4-hour window dedicated entirely to modifications, testing, and perfecting the 
high-stakes 3-minute demo. 
 


----- PAGE 221 -----
Based on the comprehensive OpsHub document and your specific constraints, here is a 
highly optimized division of labor for a 4-person team in a 24-hour hackathon. 
This structure ensures that Frontend and Backend are developed in parallel, Features are 
strictly isolated to avoid merge conflicts, Automation is handled properly, and Integration is 
exclusively managed by a single member to prevent duplicate wiring and chaotic state 
management. 
It also includes a structured timeline to guarantee buffer time for modifications, bug fixes, 
and the mandatory 5x demo rehearsal. 
👤 Member 1: The Integrator & DevOps Lead (The "Glue") 
Role: Sole owner of Integration, Automation, and DevOps. This member does not build the 
isolated UI components or the core database logic; instead, they wire everything together, 
manage the state, and automate the pipelines. 
●​ Integration (Exclusive Owner): 
○​ Stitches the Frontend (React/Zustand) to the Backend (Express/Drizzle). 
○​ Writes all API call layers (Axios instances, error interceptors). 
○​ Passes the JWT tokens, handles the 10-minute expiry/refresh flow, and 
manages the global state (site switcher). 
○​ Wires the JSON config from the Builder (Member 3) to the Universal 
Renderer (Member 4). 
●​ Automation: 
○​ Writes the CI/CD pipeline (GitHub Actions) for the "slug grep" check and test 
runner. 
○​ Creates the 1-command Docker Compose setup. 
○​ Writes and tests the npm run demo:reset script (critical for rehearsing the 
demo). 
●​ Backend Support: 
○​ Sets up the Express server shell, middleware (rate limiting, auth check, body 
validation), and idempotency keys. 
●​ Feature Ownership: One-Save Provisioning (S5) – orchestrating the backend site 
creation function with the frontend real-time log. 
👤 Member 2: The Data & Security Architect (Backend Heavy) 
Role: The core backend brain. Handles everything related to the database, isolation, 
transactions, and security. 
●​ Backend Coding: 
○​ Sets up Postgres 16, Drizzle ORM, and the base migrations. 
○​ Implements the crucial Row Level Security (RLS) policies (app_user, 
NOBYPASSRLS). 
○​ Writes the withTenant / scoped(tx) wrappers to ensure site_id is 
injected into every query. 


----- PAGE 222 -----
●​ Feature Ownership: 
○​ Transactional Stock (M7): Writes the complex, deadlock-free inventory 
assignment logic (sorting lines by item ID, locking rows FOR UPDATE). 
○​ Trust Console (S2): Builds the isolation proofs, the 50-concurrent-requests 
endpoint, and the dot-grid query. 
○​ Excel Engine (M9 & M13): Builds the secure import/export endpoints, 
enforcing limits and preventing formula injection. 
●​ Testing: Writes the backend isolation matrix, pool leak tests, and idempotency tests. 
👤 Member 3: The Brain & Builder (Frontend Heavy) 
Role: The visual architect. Builds the complex interactive tools used by the Hub Admin to 
design and configure sites. 
●​ Frontend Coding: 
○​ Builds the Hub UI pages (/hub, /hub/sites, /hub/builder). 
○​ Implements the Typed Canvas using React Flow (node palette, typed edges, 
subtype validation). 
○​ Sets up the Theme Engine (CSS variables mapping from config). 
●​ Feature Ownership: 
○​ OpsHub Compiler: Writes the Zod + jsep logic that turns the visual graph 
into validated JSON and parses mathematical formulas securely. 
○​ Bolo & Build (S3): Integrates the LLM patch generation, the voice API (Web 
Speech API for Hindi/Marathi/English), and the fallback JSON. 
○​ Release Rewind (S1): Builds the time slider, the plain-English diff generator, 
and the "Impact-aware publish" modal. 
●​ Testing: Writes the graph validation tests (ensuring cyclical loops or bad connections 
fail) and expression tampering tests. 
👤 Member 4: The Runtime Specialist (Fullstack Business Logic) 
Role: The engine driver. Takes the JSON configuration outputted by Member 3 and turns it 
into working business pages. 
●​ Frontend & Backend Coding: 
○​ Builds the Universal Renderer that reads the active release and draws the 
layouts (Table, Kanban, Split, Dashboard). 
○​ Builds the 13 frozen modules (Requests, Approvals, Assignments, 
Consumption, Returns, PCB Repair). 
●​ Feature Ownership: 
○​ Dead-Zone Lite (S4): Configures vite-plugin-pwa, the IndexedDB 
caching layer, and the offline banner. 
○​ Dynamic UI Elements: Builds the Aging SLA rings, the Ctrl+K cross-site 
search palette, and Recharts analytics dashboards. 
●​ Testing: Writes the module routing tests (ensuring disabled modules return 404/hide 
from UI) and offline pre-warming tests. 


----- PAGE 223 -----
